A group service has to decrypt each message to pass it on to the members, so whoever runs it can read that group. So a group runs its own service, on one member's machine, and never on a nest "for everyone". Video is left out on purpose: no streaming. Android is covered; the iPhone is still open.

Un service de groupe doit déchiffrer chaque message pour le transmettre aux membres : celui qui le fait tourner peut donc lire ce groupe. Un groupe fait donc tourner son propre service, sur la machine d'un de ses membres, et jamais sur un nid « pour tout le monde ». La vidéo est laissée de côté exprès : pas de streaming. Android est couvert ; l'iPhone reste ouvert.

Un servicio de grupo tiene que descifrar cada mensaje para pasárselo a los miembros: quien lo lleva puede leer ese grupo. Por eso un grupo lleva su propio servicio, en la máquina de uno de sus miembros, y nunca en un nido «para todos». El vídeo se deja fuera a propósito: sin streaming. Android está cubierto; el iPhone sigue abierto.

IPv6 without DHCPIPv6 sans DHCPIPv6 sin DHCP

IPv4 needs DHCP because its addresses are scarce: something has to hand them out and remember who holds which. An IPv6 link has 264 addresses, so each device picks its own.

IPv4 a besoin de DHCP parce que ses adresses sont rares : il faut bien quelque chose pour les distribuer et se souvenir de qui tient laquelle. Un lien IPv6 a 264 adresses, donc chaque appareil choisit la sienne.

IPv4 necesita DHCP porque sus direcciones escasean: algo tiene que repartirlas y recordar quién tiene cuál. Un enlace IPv6 tiene 264 direcciones, así que cada aparato elige la suya.

  1. Link-local first. Every interface makes an fe80:: address on its own, with no router at all. That is the recovery path.
  2. The device asks the link whether a router is there (router solicitation).
  3. A nest answers with a router advertisement: the village prefix, and the DNS server (RDNSS).
  4. The device builds its address: the prefix plus 64 bits of its own.
  5. It checks that nobody else is using it (duplicate address detection).
  1. D'abord le lien-local. Chaque interface se fabrique seule une adresse fe80::, sans aucun routeur. C'est le chemin de secours.
  2. L'appareil demande au lien s'il y a un routeur (sollicitation de routeur).
  3. Un nid répond par une annonce de routeur : le préfixe du village, et le serveur DNS (RDNSS).
  4. L'appareil construit son adresse : le préfixe plus 64 bits à lui.
  5. Il vérifie que personne d'autre ne l'utilise (détection d'adresse dupliquée).
  1. Primero el enlace local. Cada interfaz se hace sola una dirección fe80::, sin ningún router. Es el camino de recuperación.
  2. El aparato pregunta al enlace si hay un router (solicitud de router).
  3. Un nido responde con un anuncio de router: el prefijo del pueblo y el servidor DNS (RDNSS).
  4. El aparato construye su dirección: el prefijo más 64 bits suyos.
  5. Comprueba que nadie más la usa (detección de direcciones duplicadas).

Nothing keeps a list of who has which address: there is nothing to lose, to sync between two nests, or to log. Android never speaks DHCPv6 and takes its DNS only from the RA, so this is also the one way that covers every phone.

Rien ne tient la liste de qui a quelle adresse : rien à perdre, rien à synchroniser entre deux nids, rien à journaliser. Android ne parle jamais DHCPv6 et ne prend son DNS que dans le RA : c'est donc aussi la seule méthode qui couvre tous les téléphones.

Nada lleva la lista de quién tiene qué dirección: no hay nada que perder, que sincronizar entre dos nidos ni que registrar. Android nunca habla DHCPv6 y solo toma su DNS del RA, así que este es también el único método que cubre todos los teléfonos.

What an Android phone needs before it staysCe qu'il faut à un téléphone Android pour resterLo que un teléfono Android necesita para quedarse tested

A laptop is happy with any address. Android is not: on a Wi-Fi with no IPv4, it keeps the network only if it gets, all at once, an address it counts as global, a default router, and a DNS server. Two of these failed on the bench on 2026-11-14, and each had its own symptom.

Un portable se contente de n'importe quelle adresse. Android, non : sur un Wi-Fi sans IPv4, il ne garde le réseau que s'il obtient, tout à la fois, une adresse qu'il juge globale, un routeur par défaut et un serveur DNS. Deux de ces conditions ont échoué sur le banc le 2026-11-14, chacune avec son symptôme.

Un portátil se conforma con cualquier dirección. Android no: en un Wi-Fi sin IPv4, solo conserva la red si obtiene, todo a la vez, una dirección que considera global, un router por defecto y un servidor DNS. Dos de estas cosas fallaron en el banco el 2026-11-14, cada una con su síntoma.

  • "Obtaining IP address" forever: Android doesn't count a ULA address (fd…) as global, so a ULA-only network never counts as set up. Some versions also drop a ULA DNS server. Fix: the nest announces a second, non-ULA prefix next to the ULA, and lists its address in that prefix first as DNS server. On the bench that prefix is 2001:db8:1::/64, the range reserved for documentation, which belongs to nobody and must never go on a roof.
  • "IP configuration failure" after half a second: Android checks its default router, and a nest with forwarding off answers that check without the router flag. Android then removes the default route and leaves. Fix: net.ipv6.conf.eth0.forwarding=1 on the nest. The kernel then sets the router flag on that interface, while all.forwarding=0 and the nftables forward drop still forward nothing.
  • Result: "connected / no Internet", which is what we want. The phone keeps using 4G for the Internet.
  • « Obtention de l'adresse IP » sans fin : Android ne compte pas une adresse ULA (fd…) comme globale, donc un réseau tout en ULA n'est jamais considéré comme prêt. Certaines versions jettent aussi un serveur DNS en ULA. Correction : le nid annonce un deuxième préfixe, non-ULA, à côté de l'ULA, et place son adresse dans ce préfixe en premier comme serveur DNS. Sur le banc, ce préfixe est 2001:db8:1::/64, la plage réservée à la documentation, qui n'appartient à personne et ne doit jamais aller sur un toit.
  • « Échec de la configuration IP » au bout d'une demi-seconde : Android vérifie son routeur par défaut, et un nid avec le routage coupé répond à cette vérification sans le drapeau routeur. Android retire alors la route par défaut et s'en va. Correction : net.ipv6.conf.eth0.forwarding=1 sur le nid. Le noyau lève alors le drapeau routeur sur cette interface, tandis que all.forwarding=0 et le forward en drop de nftables ne routent toujours rien.
  • Résultat : « connecté / pas d'Internet », ce qu'on veut. Le téléphone continue d'utiliser la 4G pour Internet.
  • «Obteniendo dirección IP» sin fin: Android no cuenta una dirección ULA (fd…) como global, así que una red solo con ULA nunca se da por configurada. Algunas versiones también descartan un servidor DNS en ULA. Arreglo: el nido anuncia un segundo prefijo, no ULA, junto al ULA, y pone su dirección en ese prefijo la primera como servidor DNS. En el banco ese prefijo es 2001:db8:1::/64, el rango reservado para documentación, que no es de nadie y nunca debe subir a un tejado.
  • «Error de configuración IP» al cabo de medio segundo: Android comprueba su router por defecto, y un nido con el reenvío apagado responde a esa comprobación sin la marca de router. Android quita entonces la ruta por defecto y se va. Arreglo: net.ipv6.conf.eth0.forwarding=1 en el nido. El núcleo levanta entonces la marca de router en esa interfaz, mientras all.forwarding=0 y el forward en drop de nftables siguen sin reenviar nada.
  • Resultado: «conectado / sin Internet», que es lo que queremos. El teléfono sigue usando el 4G para Internet.

Open: which non-ULA prefix for real villages. A /64 from a VPS would work for village 1, but every phone would then carry an address that points to one person's contract and name (P5), and the village would depend on that contract (line IV). A /48 held by the project, from a LIR or a sponsoring association and never routed to the Internet, fits the address plan: one /64 per village, next to the ULA. Not decided.

Ouvert : quel préfixe non-ULA pour les vrais villages. Un /64 pris sur un VPS marcherait pour le village 1, mais chaque téléphone porterait alors une adresse qui renvoie au contrat et au nom d'une seule personne (P5), et le village dépendrait de ce contrat (ligne IV). Un /48 détenu par le projet, obtenu auprès d'un LIR ou d'une association parrain et jamais routé vers Internet, colle au plan d'adressage : un /64 par village, à côté de l'ULA. Pas décidé.

Abierto: qué prefijo no ULA para los pueblos reales. Un /64 de un VPS serviría para el pueblo 1, pero cada teléfono llevaría entonces una dirección que apunta al contrato y al nombre de una persona (P5), y el pueblo dependería de ese contrato (línea IV). Un /48 del proyecto, obtenido de un LIR o de una asociación que lo apadrine y nunca enrutado a Internet, encaja con el plan de direcciones: un /64 por pueblo, junto al ULA. Sin decidir.

With no nest at allSans aucun nidSin ningún nido

  • Still works: the backbone (layer 2, no IP involved), link-local addresses across the whole mesh, so every station stays reachable for admin.
  • Should still work: Reticulum between devices, since its peer discovery uses link-local multicast. not yet tested
  • Stops: new addresses and DNS, so the welcome page and the captive portal. Addresses already built expire with the RA lifetimes.
  • Likely: an Android phone leaves a Wi-Fi that gives it no RA, since it then has no global address and no router (see above). not yet tested
  • Marche encore : la dorsale (couche 2, sans IP), les adresses lien-local sur tout le mesh : chaque station reste joignable pour l'admin.
  • Devrait encore marcher : Reticulum entre appareils, puisque sa découverte des pairs passe par le multicast lien-local. not yet tested
  • S'arrête : les nouvelles adresses et le DNS, donc la page d'accueil et le portail captif. Les adresses déjà construites expirent avec les durées de vie des RA.
  • Probable : un téléphone Android quitte un Wi-Fi qui ne lui donne aucun RA, puisqu'il n'a alors ni adresse globale ni routeur (voir plus haut). not yet tested
  • Sigue funcionando: la troncal (capa 2, sin IP), las direcciones de enlace local en toda la malla: cada estación sigue siendo alcanzable para administrarla.
  • Debería seguir funcionando: Reticulum entre aparatos, ya que su descubrimiento de pares usa multidifusión de enlace local. not yet tested
  • Se para: las direcciones nuevas y el DNS, y con ellos la página de bienvenida y el portal cautivo. Las direcciones ya construidas caducan con los tiempos de vida de los RA.
  • Probable: un teléfono Android abandona un Wi-Fi que no le da ningún RA, porque entonces no tiene ni dirección global ni router (ver arriba). not yet tested

III. How to build itIII. Comment le construireIII. Cómo construirla

Each section below is a worked example of one role on one piece of hardware. Other hardware takes the same role if it runs the same standard software. Commands and config files are the same in every language, so they appear once. Comments inside them stay in English.

Chaque section ci-dessous est un exemple concret d'un rôle sur un matériel. Un autre matériel prend le même rôle s'il fait tourner le même logiciel standard. Les commandes et les fichiers de configuration sont les mêmes dans toutes les langues : ils n'apparaissent qu'une fois. Leurs commentaires restent en anglais.

Cada sección de abajo es un ejemplo concreto de un papel en un hardware. Otro hardware toma el mismo papel si hace funcionar el mismo software estándar. Las órdenes y los archivos de configuración son los mismos en todos los idiomas, así que aparecen una sola vez. Sus comentarios se quedan en inglés.

The scripts are separate files that travel with this handbook: sparrows-openwrt/ builds the station images (build.sh, profiles.txt, uses.txt, one first-boot file for every role) and tao/ builds this page (build.py). They are printed here in full, straight from those files, so they can be read before they are run. Download them from the kit folder next to this page (how it is made).

Les scripts sont des fichiers séparés qui voyagent avec ce manuel : sparrows-openwrt/ construit les images des stations (build.sh, profiles.txt, uses.txt, un seul fichier de premier démarrage pour tous les rôles) et tao/ construit cette page (build.py). Ils sont imprimés ici en entier, directement depuis ces fichiers, pour pouvoir les lire avant de les lancer. Les télécharger dans le dossier kit à côté de cette page (comment il est fait).

Los scripts son archivos aparte que viajan con este manual: sparrows-openwrt/ construye las imágenes de las estaciones (build.sh, profiles.txt, uses.txt, un solo archivo de primer arranque para todos los papeles) y tao/ construye esta página (build.py). Se imprimen aquí enteros, directamente desde esos archivos, para poder leerlos antes de ejecutarlos. Descargarlos de la carpeta kit junto a esta página (cómo se hace).

Station images: perch, feeder, hedge, tree, nestImages des stations : perchoir, mangeoire, haie, arbre, nidImágenes de las estaciones: percha, comedero, seto, árbol, nido not yet tested

One build tree, sparrows-openwrt, makes one image per role and per device with the OpenWrt Image Builder. Every unit of a role gets the same image: nothing to set per unit. Written on 2026-11-13, hedge added on 2026-11-14, tree on 2026-11-19. So far the hedge image has run on one EAP225-Outdoor v3, flashed from stock firmware 5.1.0: it boots, takes SSH keys over link-local, brings up both radios and the mesh interface, passes the radar check, and lets a laptop and a phone into the village (2026-11-14). The perch, feeder and tree images have not run on a device yet.

Un seul arbre de build, sparrows-openwrt, fabrique une image par rôle et par appareil avec l'Image Builder d'OpenWrt. Tous les boîtiers d'un rôle reçoivent la même image : rien à régler par boîtier. Écrit le 2026-11-13, haie ajoutée le 2026-11-14, arbre le 2026-11-19. À ce jour, l'image de haie a tourné sur un EAP225-Outdoor v3, flashé depuis le firmware d'origine 5.1.0 : il démarre, accepte les clés SSH en lien-local, monte ses deux radios et l'interface mesh, passe le test radar, et fait entrer un portable et un téléphone dans le village (2026-11-14). Les images de perchoir, de mangeoire et d'arbre n'ont pas encore tourné sur un appareil.

Un solo árbol de construcción, sparrows-openwrt, hace una imagen por papel y por aparato con el Image Builder de OpenWrt. Todas las unidades de un papel reciben la misma imagen: nada que ajustar por unidad. Escrito el 2026-11-13, seto añadido el 2026-11-14, árbol el 2026-11-19. Hasta hoy la imagen de seto ha funcionado en un EAP225-Outdoor v3, flasheado desde el firmware original 5.1.0: arranca, acepta claves SSH por enlace local, levanta las dos radios y la interfaz de malla, pasa la comprobación de radar y deja entrar en el pueblo a un portátil y a un teléfono (2026-11-14). Las imágenes de percha, comedero y árbol aún no han funcionado en ningún aparato.

  • Name: each unit names itself after its role and the end of its MAC, for example perch-3fa2, feeder-91c0 or hedge-07be. A unit with two roles takes both names: nest-feeder-26de.
  • Address: SLAAC from the nests' RAs, built from the MAC on the sticker, plus link-local. The unit receives RAs and never sends any. A nest sets the same address itself, from the same MAC, and sends the RAs.
  • Admin: SSH keys only, root password locked. Failsafe mode still works for recovery.
  • Never: IPv4, DHCP, a firewall, a link to the Internet. RAs only from a nest. This part is shared by every role. Reticulum only where the role asks for it: tree and nest.
  • Secrets: an image with a 5 GHz flyway (perch) carries the flyway key, one with a 2.4 GHz flyway (hedge) the hedge key. build.sh refuses a key that isn't one line of 8 to 63 printable ASCII characters.
  • Other hardware: one line in profiles.txt, with the power to set at the radio on each band: the legal EIRP minus the antenna gain. The build menu can write that line: n, then the target from the device's OpenWrt page, part of the model name, the role and the two powers. The line is marked "not yet tested" until the device has run on the bench. A device that needs more than a line (another OpenWrt profile, firmware to fetch, its own first-boot file) gets a folder in devices/, like the Pi Zero 2 W.
  • Nom : chaque boîtier se nomme d'après son rôle et la fin de sa MAC, par exemple perch-3fa2, feeder-91c0 ou hedge-07be. Un boîtier à deux rôles prend les deux noms : nest-feeder-26de.
  • Adresse : SLAAC à partir des RA des nids, construite à partir de la MAC de l'étiquette, plus le lien-local. Le boîtier reçoit des RA et n'en envoie jamais. Un nid se donne lui-même la même adresse, à partir de la même MAC, et envoie les RA.
  • Admin : clés SSH seulement, mot de passe root verrouillé. Le mode failsafe marche toujours pour le dépannage.
  • Jamais : IPv4, DHCP, pare-feu, lien vers Internet. Des RA seulement depuis un nid. Cette partie est commune à tous les rôles. Reticulum seulement là où le rôle le demande : arbre et nid.
  • Secrets : une image avec une voie 5 GHz (perchoir) porte la clé de la voie, une image avec une voie 2,4 GHz (haie) la clé des haies. build.sh refuse une clé qui n'est pas une seule ligne de 8 à 63 caractères ASCII imprimables.
  • Autre matériel : une ligne dans profiles.txt, avec la puissance à régler sur la radio pour chaque bande : la PIRE légale moins le gain d'antenne. Le menu de build peut écrire cette ligne : n, puis la cible donnée par la page OpenWrt de l'appareil, une partie du nom du modèle, le rôle et les deux puissances. La ligne est marquée « not yet tested » tant que l'appareil n'a pas tourné sur le banc. Un appareil qui demande plus qu'une ligne (un autre profil OpenWrt, un firmware à récupérer, son propre fichier de premier démarrage) a un dossier dans devices/, comme le Pi Zero 2 W.
  • Nombre: cada unidad se pone nombre según su papel y el final de su MAC, por ejemplo perch-3fa2, feeder-91c0 o hedge-07be. Una unidad con dos papeles toma los dos nombres: nest-feeder-26de.
  • Dirección: SLAAC a partir de los RA de los nidos, construida con la MAC de la etiqueta, más el enlace local. La unidad recibe RA y nunca envía ninguno. Un nido se pone él mismo la misma dirección, a partir de la misma MAC, y envía los RA.
  • Administración: solo claves SSH, contraseña de root bloqueada. El modo failsafe sigue sirviendo para recuperar.
  • Nunca: IPv4, DHCP, cortafuegos, enlace a Internet. RA solo desde un nido. Esta parte es común a todos los papeles. Reticulum solo donde el papel lo pide: árbol y nido.
  • Secretos: una imagen con ruta de 5 GHz (percha) lleva la clave de la ruta, una con ruta de 2,4 GHz (seto) la clave de los setos. build.sh rechaza una clave que no sea una sola línea de 8 a 63 caracteres ASCII imprimibles.
  • Otro hardware: una línea en profiles.txt, con la potencia que poner en la radio en cada banda: la PIRE legal menos la ganancia de la antena. El menú de construcción puede escribir esa línea: n, luego el target que da la página OpenWrt del aparato, parte del nombre del modelo, el papel y las dos potencias. La línea queda marcada «not yet tested» hasta que el aparato haya funcionado en el banco. Un aparato que necesita más que una línea (otro perfil OpenWrt, un firmware que descargar, su propio archivo de primer arranque) tiene una carpeta en devices/, como la Pi Zero 2 W.

What each port and radio doesCe que fait chaque port et chaque radioLo que hace cada puerto y cada radio not yet tested

Decided on 2026-11-21. A role is a preset of uses, one per interface, written in uses.txt. One first-boot file, 90-sparrows-station, reads them and sets up every role; it replaces the four per-role files, with the same radio settings. The uses:

Décidé le 2026-11-21. Un rôle est un préréglage d'usages, un par interface, écrit dans uses.txt. Un seul fichier de premier démarrage, 90-sparrows-station, les lit et monte chaque rôle ; il remplace les quatre fichiers par rôle, avec les mêmes réglages radio. Les usages :

Decidido el 2026-11-21. Un papel es un ajuste previo de usos, uno por interfaz, escrito en uses.txt. Un solo archivo de primer arranque, 90-sparrows-station, los lee y monta cada papel; sustituye a los cuatro archivos por papel, con los mismos ajustes de radio. Los usos:

  • wire: the Ethernet ports, a cable to the site's switch, part of the village segment.
  • feeder: an access point of the open Sparrows Wi-Fi.
  • flyway: a radio link to other stations carrying batman-adv. On 5 GHz, AP + client as on a perch; on 2.4 GHz, the 802.11s mesh of the hedges.
  • join: a client of the Sparrows Wi-Fi, like a phone. Never bridged.
  • off, and two services: reticulum (a tree) and nest.
  • fil (wire) : les ports Ethernet, un câble vers le switch du site, partie du segment du village.
  • mangeoire (feeder) : un point d'accès du Wi-Fi ouvert Sparrows.
  • voie (flyway) : un lien radio vers d'autres stations qui transporte batman-adv. En 5 GHz, AP + client comme sur un perchoir ; en 2,4 GHz, le mesh 802.11s des haies.
  • rejoindre (join) : un client du Wi-Fi Sparrows, comme un téléphone. Jamais ponté.
  • éteint (off), et deux services : reticulum (un arbre) et nest (un nid).
  • hilo (wire): los puertos Ethernet, un cable al switch del sitio, parte del segmento del pueblo.
  • comedero (feeder): un punto de acceso del Wi-Fi abierto Sparrows.
  • ruta (flyway): un enlace de radio hacia otras estaciones que lleva batman-adv. En 5 GHz, AP + cliente como en una percha; en 2,4 GHz, la malla 802.11s de los setos.
  • unirse (join): un cliente del Wi-Fi Sparrows, como un teléfono. Nunca en el puente.
  • apagado (off), y dos servicios: reticulum (un árbol) y nest (un nido).

Roles add up on one unit when it has the room: tree+feeder or nest+feeder in the role column of profiles.txt takes the uses of both. A radio that would both join and serve doesn't join, and a nest never joins. A device that needs something else gets a manual line, uses=wire,feeder,flyway,- (ports, 2.4 GHz, 5 GHz, services), written by the menu: n, then m. The build refuses what can't work and says why, before downloading anything: a use the chip can't do (no mesh on a Pi's Wi-Fi, from devices/*/device.conf), a flyway and a feeder on one 5 GHz radio (two channels), a unit left with nothing to do. Before building it prints what each port and radio will do; ./build.sh uses prints only that. Tried on 2026-11-21 against a stand-in Image Builder and a stand-in uci: each role gets the same settings as with its old file. Not yet run on a unit.

Les rôles s'additionnent sur un boîtier quand il a la place : tree+feeder ou nest+feeder dans la colonne rôle de profiles.txt prend les usages des deux. Une radio qui devrait à la fois rejoindre et servir ne rejoint pas, et un nid ne rejoint jamais. Un appareil qui demande autre chose reçoit une ligne manuelle, uses=wire,feeder,flyway,- (ports, 2,4 GHz, 5 GHz, services), écrite par le menu : n, puis m. Le build refuse ce qui ne peut pas marcher et dit pourquoi, avant de télécharger quoi que ce soit : un usage que la puce ne sait pas faire (pas de mesh sur le Wi-Fi d'un Pi, d'après devices/*/device.conf), une voie et une mangeoire sur une seule radio 5 GHz (deux canaux), un boîtier qui n'aurait plus rien à faire. Avant de construire, il affiche ce que fera chaque port et chaque radio ; ./build.sh uses n'affiche que cela. Essayé le 2026-11-21 avec un faux Image Builder et un faux uci : chaque rôle reçoit les mêmes réglages qu'avec son ancien fichier. Pas encore lancé sur un boîtier.

Los papeles se suman en una unidad cuando tiene sitio: tree+feeder o nest+feeder en la columna de papel de profiles.txt toma los usos de los dos. Una radio que debería a la vez unirse y servir no se une, y un nido nunca se une. Un aparato que necesita otra cosa recibe una línea manual, uses=wire,feeder,flyway,- (puertos, 2,4 GHz, 5 GHz, servicios), escrita por el menú: n, luego m. La construcción rechaza lo que no puede funcionar y dice por qué, antes de descargar nada: un uso que el chip no sabe hacer (sin malla en el Wi-Fi de una Pi, según devices/*/device.conf), una ruta y un comedero en una sola radio de 5 GHz (dos canales), una unidad que se quedaría sin nada que hacer. Antes de construir muestra lo que hará cada puerto y cada radio; ./build.sh uses solo muestra eso. Probado el 2026-11-21 con un Image Builder falso y un uci falso: cada papel recibe los mismos ajustes que con su archivo antiguo. Aún no lanzado en una unidad.

uses.txt
# Sparrows-Network: what each port and radio of a station does, by role. Read by build.sh.
# A role is a preset of uses, one per interface. To change what a role does, change its line here.
#
# Uses of a port or a radio:
#   wire     the Ethernet ports: a cable to the site's switch, part of the village segment (bridged)
#   feeder   an access point of the open "Sparrows" Wi-Fi, for people's devices (bridged)
#   flyway   a radio link to other stations, carrying batman-adv (bridged):
#              5 GHz   AP + client, hidden, SAE (secrets/flyway.key), ch 108; `perch ap` picks the AP
#              2.4 GHz 802.11s mesh to the other units on the same channel, SAE (secrets/hedge.key), ch 1
#   join     a client of the "Sparrows" Wi-Fi, like a phone. Never bridged: nothing hangs behind it
#   off      not used
#   Several uses on one radio: joined with +, e.g. flyway+feeder
# Services:
#   reticulum  rnsd with transport + the Sparrows page (Nomad Network). Needs room: SD-card devices
#   nest       Reticulum, plus RAs (odhcpd), DNS (dnsmasq, no DHCP), the welcome page (uhttpd) and
#              the message store (lxmd), on a fixed village address from the MAC. The only unit that
#              sends RAs. Never joins: its radios serve
#   -          none
#
# Roles add up on one unit: tree+feeder takes the uses of both. When a radio would both join and do
# something else, it doesn't join: Reticulum then runs on the village bridge (wire + feeders).
# Only one radio joins: when both would, the 2.4 GHz one does and the 5 GHz one is off.
# One 5 GHz radio can't hold a flyway and a feeder (two channels): the build refuses it.
# What a device's radios can't do (no mesh on a Pi's Wi-Fi, say) is in devices/<it>/device.conf.
# A band set to - in profiles.txt is off whatever the role says.
#
# role    ports  2.4GHz         5GHz     services
perch     wire   off            flyway   -
feeder    wire   feeder         feeder   -
hedge     wire   flyway+feeder  feeder   -
tree      wire   join           join     reticulum
nest      wire   off            off      nest
station/files/etc/uci-defaults/90-sparrows-station
#!/bin/sh
# Sparrows-Network: what each port and radio of this station does. One file for every role.
# build.sh writes the uses in /etc/sparrows/station, from uses.txt (or a manual line of profiles.txt):
#   PORTS = wire | off              USE2, USE5 = uses of the 2.4 / 5 GHz radio: off, or a list of
#   RETICULUM, NEST = 1 | 0         feeder (open Wi-Fi), flyway (batman-adv link), join (Wi-Fi client)
# Bridge only: no IPv4, no DHCP, no RA, no firewall (base in 10-sparrows-common). A nest adds its
# services after this file (91-sparrows-nest).
# Runs once at first boot (and again after `firstboot && reboot`).
# Status: NOT YET TESTED. Replaces the per-role files (2026-11-21), with the same radio settings.

. /etc/sparrows/station

# The radio settings (SSIDs, channels, keys) are in /usr/lib/sparrows/radios
has() { case " $1 " in *" $2 "*) return 0 ;; esac; return 1; }
ALL="$USE2 $USE5"


# --- Ports: wire = in the village bridge (the image's default); off = out of it ---
BR=$(uci show network | sed -n "s/^network\.\([^.]*\)\.name='br-lan'$/\1/p")
[ "$PORTS" != off ] || uci -q delete network.$BR.ports

# --- Flyway: batman-adv, bridged with the rest in br-lan ---
if has "$ALL" flyway; then
	uci set network.bat0=interface
	uci set network.bat0.proto='batadv'
	uci set network.bat0.routing_algo='BATMAN_IV'
	uci set network.bat0.bridge_loop_avoidance='1'   # a site switch with 2 batman nodes = two paths;
	                                                 # BLA must be on all of them
	uci set network.mesh0=interface
	uci set network.mesh0.proto='batadv_hardif'
	uci set network.mesh0.master='bat0'
	uci set network.mesh0.mtu='1536'                 # batman header, avoids fragmentation
	uci -q del_list network.$BR.ports='bat0'
	uci add_list network.$BR.ports='bat0'
fi

# --- Join: the Wi-Fi client is an interface of its own, address by SLAAC from the nests' RAs ---
if has "$ALL" join; then
	uci set network.wsta=interface
	uci set network.wsta.proto='dhcpv6'
	uci set network.wsta.reqaddress='none'
	uci set network.wsta.reqprefix='no'
fi
uci commit network

# --- Radios: /usr/lib/sparrows/radios. Run here when the radios are already there (most routers),
# and again by hotplug when a radio shows up later: a Raspberry Pi's Wi-Fi chip appears seconds after
# this file has run (seen 2026-11-21: the Pi 4 nest kept OpenWrt's default Wi-Fi, never ours) ---
/usr/lib/sparrows/radios

# --- Reticulum: on the Wi-Fi client if a radio joins, else on the village bridge ---
if [ "$RETICULUM" = 1 ]; then
	if has "$ALL" join; then DEV='wsta'; else DEV='br-lan'; fi
	sed -i "s|@RNSDEV@|$DEV|" /etc/reticulum/config
	. /lib/functions.sh
	group_add rns 1500 2>/dev/null
	user_add rns 1500 1500 rns /etc/reticulum /bin/false 2>/dev/null   # its own user, never root
	sed -i "s|@NAME@|$(uci -q get system.@system[0].hostname)|" /etc/nomadnetwork/config
	chown -R rns:rns /etc/reticulum /etc/nomadnetwork
	/etc/init.d/rnsd enable
	/etc/init.d/nomadnet enable
fi

exit 0

The radios are set by a script of their own, run at first boot and again by hotplug when a radio shows up later: a Raspberry Pi's Wi-Fi chip appears a few seconds after first boot has run (seen 2026-11-21). Once done it leaves /etc/sparrows/radios.done; delete that file to set the radios again from the uses.

Les radios sont réglées par un script à part, lancé au premier démarrage et de nouveau par hotplug quand une radio apparaît plus tard : la puce Wi-Fi d'un Raspberry Pi apparaît quelques secondes après le premier démarrage (vu le 2026-11-21). Une fois fait, il laisse /etc/sparrows/radios.done ; supprimer ce fichier pour régler à nouveau les radios d'après les usages.

Las radios las ajusta un script aparte, lanzado en el primer arranque y otra vez por hotplug cuando una radio aparece más tarde: el chip Wi-Fi de una Raspberry Pi aparece unos segundos después del primer arranque (visto el 2026-11-21). Al terminar deja /etc/sparrows/radios.done; borrar ese archivo para volver a ajustar las radios según los usos.

station/files/usr/lib/sparrows/radios
#!/bin/sh
# Sparrows-Network: the radios of this station, from its uses (/etc/sparrows/station: USE2, USE5, TX2, TX5).
# Run by 90-sparrows-station at first boot, and by hotplug (/etc/hotplug.d/ieee80211/20-sparrows-radios)
# when a radio shows up after that, as on a Raspberry Pi. Once the radios are set, it writes
# /etc/sparrows/radios.done and does nothing more: to redo it, delete that file (or firstboot).
# Status: NOT YET TESTED on a unit (2026-11-21).

[ -f /etc/sparrows/radios.done ] && exit 0
. /etc/sparrows/station

SSID='Sparrows'                  # the open Wi-Fi, for people's devices
FLYWAY_SSID='wifilibre-link'     # 5 GHz flyway: same as the running one, so new perches join it
FLYWAY_CH='108'                  # outdoor FR: 100-140 (DFS), never 120-128 (weather radar)
MESH_ID='sparrows-hedge'         # 2.4 GHz flyway: same on every unit of the village
MESH_CH='1'                      # fixed, same on every unit (a mesh can't use ACS): 1, 6 or 11
FEED_CH2="${CH2:-auto}"          # 2.4 GHz feeder alone: ACS, unless the device sets one (no ACS)
FEED_CH5='132'                   # 5 GHz feeder: outdoor DFS, VHT40 = 132+136. Far from 108

has() { case " $1 " in *" $2 "*) return 0 ;; esac; return 1; }

# A radio key: 8-63 printable ASCII, or SAE never links (seen on the flyway 2026-11-14, Pitfalls).
# build.sh already refuses a bad key; this only catches a hand-edited one.
key() {
	chmod 600 "/etc/sparrows/$1"
	K=$(cat "/etc/sparrows/$1")
	printf '%s' "$K" | LC_ALL=C grep -qxE '[ -~]{8,63}' || \
		logger -p daemon.err -t sparrows "$1 is not 8-63 printable ASCII: this flyway will not link"
	printf '%s' "$K"
}

# --- Radios: the first radio of each band takes that band's uses, any other is off ---
[ -s /etc/config/wireless ] || wifi config
RADIOS=$(uci -q show wireless | sed -n 's/^wireless\.\([^.=]*\)=wifi-device$/\1/p')
[ -n "$RADIOS" ] || exit 0           # no radio yet: hotplug runs this again when one appears
# A radio that can do two bands gets the highest one from OpenWrt (seen 2026-11-21 on a Pi 4: 5 GHz,
# so a 2.4 GHz-only feeder came up with its radio off). A band that has uses but no radio takes a
# radio whose own band has none.
for B in 2g 5g; do
	if [ "$B" = 2g ]; then U=$USE2 TX=$TX2; else U=$USE5 TX=$TX5; fi
	{ [ -n "$U" ] && [ "$TX" != '-' ]; } || continue
	FOUND=''
	for r in $RADIOS; do [ "$(uci -q get wireless.$r.band)" != "$B" ] || FOUND=$r; done
	[ -z "$FOUND" ] || continue
	for r in $RADIOS; do
		case "$(uci -q get wireless.$r.band)" in
			2g) RU=$USE2 RT=$TX2 ;;
			5g) RU=$USE5 RT=$TX5 ;;
			*)  RU='' RT='-' ;;
		esac
		if [ -z "$RU" ] || [ "$RT" = '-' ]; then
			logger -t sparrows "radio $r: band $(uci -q get wireless.$r.band) has no use here, set to $B"
			uci set wireless.$r.band="$B"
			break
		fi
	done
done
DONE=''
for r in $RADIOS; do
	uci -q delete wireless.default_$r
	B=$(uci -q get wireless.$r.band)
	case "$B" in
		2g) U=$USE2; TX=$TX2 ;;
		5g) U=$USE5; TX=$TX5 ;;
		*)  U=''; TX='-' ;;
	esac
	if [ "$TX" = '-' ] || [ -z "$U" ] || [ "$U" = off ] || has "$DONE" "$B"; then
		uci set wireless.$r.disabled='1'
		continue
	fi
	DONE="$DONE $B"
	uci set wireless.$r.disabled='0'
	uci set wireless.$r.country='FR'
	uci set wireless.$r.log_level='4'          # warnings only: no per-client connect lines
	uci set wireless.$r.txpower="$TX"

	# Channel: a flyway fixes it; a client follows its AP; a feeder alone uses the feeder's
	if [ "$B" = 2g ]; then
		HT='HT20'
		if has "$U" flyway; then CH=$MESH_CH; elif has "$U" join; then CH='auto'; else CH=$FEED_CH2; fi
	else
		if has "$U" flyway; then CH=$FLYWAY_CH; HT='HT20'
		elif has "$U" join; then CH='auto'; HT='VHT40'
		else CH=$FEED_CH5; HT='VHT40'; fi
	fi
	uci set wireless.$r.channel="$CH"
	uci set wireless.$r.htmode="$HT"

	if has "$U" flyway && [ "$B" = 5g ]; then
		uci set wireless.flyway=wifi-iface       # the perch tool works on this one
		uci set wireless.flyway.device="$r"
		uci set wireless.flyway.mode='sta'       # safe default: a client only transmits after hearing its AP
		uci set wireless.flyway.ssid="$FLYWAY_SSID"
		uci set wireless.flyway.encryption='sae'
		uci set wireless.flyway.key="$(key flyway.key)"
		uci set wireless.flyway.network='mesh0'
	fi
	if has "$U" flyway && [ "$B" = 2g ]; then
		uci set wireless.mesh_$r=wifi-iface
		uci set wireless.mesh_$r.device="$r"
		uci set wireless.mesh_$r.mode='mesh'
		uci set wireless.mesh_$r.mesh_id="$MESH_ID"
		uci set wireless.mesh_$r.encryption='sae'
		uci set wireless.mesh_$r.key="$(key hedge.key)"
		uci set wireless.mesh_$r.mesh_fwding='0'     # batman-adv does the routing, not 802.11s
		uci set wireless.mesh_$r.network='mesh0'
	fi
	if has "$U" feeder; then
		uci set wireless.ap_$r=wifi-iface
		uci set wireless.ap_$r.device="$r"
		uci set wireless.ap_$r.mode='ap'
		uci set wireless.ap_$r.ssid="$SSID"
		uci set wireless.ap_$r.encryption='none'         # on-ramp: anyone can join
		uci set wireless.ap_$r.network='lan'
		uci set wireless.ap_$r.isolate='0'               # phones must reach each other (Reticulum, P3)
		uci set wireless.ap_$r.multicast_to_unicast_all='1'  # RAs + Reticulum discovery reach clients reliably
	fi
	if has "$U" join; then
		uci set wireless.wsta=wifi-iface
		uci set wireless.wsta.device="$r"
		uci set wireless.wsta.mode='sta'
		uci set wireless.wsta.ssid="$SSID"
		uci set wireless.wsta.encryption='none'          # the open on-ramp Wi-Fi, like any phone
		uci set wireless.wsta.network='wsta'
		uci set wireless.wsta.ifname='wsta'              # fixed name: the Reticulum config uses it
	fi
done
uci commit wireless
touch /etc/sparrows/radios.done
logger -t sparrows "radios set from the station's uses: 2.4 GHz '${USE2:-off}', 5 GHz '${USE5:-off}'"
station/files/etc/hotplug.d/ieee80211/20-sparrows-radios
#!/bin/sh
# Sparrows-Network: a radio that appears after first boot (a Raspberry Pi's Wi-Fi chip) gets its uses.
# Runs after OpenWrt's 10-wifi-detect, which has just written its default config for it.
[ "$ACTION" = add ] || exit 0
[ -f /etc/sparrows/radios.done ] && exit 0
[ -f /etc/sparrows/station ] || exit 0
/usr/lib/sparrows/radios && [ -f /etc/sparrows/radios.done ] && wifi reload
exit 0

1. Build1. Construire1. Construir

On any x86_64 Linux machine with Internet access.

Sur n'importe quelle machine Linux x86_64 avec un accès Internet.

En cualquier máquina Linux x86_64 con acceso a Internet.

First an admin SSH key, if there is none yet: the images accept nothing else. Then the radio keys, then the build. OpenWrt 25.12 uses apk, not opkg.

In a terminal, ./build.sh opens a menu: the devices already in profiles.txt, numbered. Pick several by number or by role (1 3, hedge), or press Enter for all. v changes the OpenWrt release, w turns the web interface on or off, s writes a Raspberry Pi image to an SD card, n adds a device. It shows the list and asks before building. From a script, ./build.sh all or a role name builds with no questions. Menu added on 2026-11-18 and tried against a stand-in Image Builder; not yet run with the real one. Checks added on 2026-11-20, tried the same way: before downloading anything it checks each chosen line of profiles.txt (a known role, a power from 0 to 30 dBm, the bands the role needs); a build replaces the images of the devices it builds and keeps the others in build/images; at the end it lists what it built and what it skipped. Since 2026-11-21 each line of the menu shows what its ports and radios do, and the build lists it for every chosen device before asking to go on; in n, m asks the use of each interface instead of a role.

D'abord une clé SSH d'admin, s'il n'y en a pas encore : les images n'acceptent rien d'autre. Puis les clés radio, puis le build. OpenWrt 25.12 utilise apk, pas opkg.

Dans un terminal, ./build.sh ouvre un menu : les appareils déjà dans profiles.txt, numérotés. On en choisit plusieurs par numéro ou par rôle (1 3, hedge), ou Entrée pour tous. v change la version d'OpenWrt, w active ou coupe l'interface web, s écrit une image Raspberry Pi sur une carte SD, n ajoute un appareil. Il montre la liste et demande avant de construire. Depuis un script, ./build.sh all ou un nom de rôle construit sans poser de question. Menu ajouté le 2026-11-18 et essayé avec un faux Image Builder ; pas encore lancé avec le vrai. Contrôles ajoutés le 2026-11-20, essayés de la même façon : avant de télécharger quoi que ce soit, il vérifie chaque ligne choisie de profiles.txt (un rôle connu, une puissance de 0 à 30 dBm, les bandes dont le rôle a besoin) ; un build remplace les images des appareils qu'il construit et garde les autres dans build/images ; à la fin il liste ce qu'il a construit et ce qu'il a sauté. Depuis le 2026-11-21, chaque ligne du menu montre ce que font ses ports et ses radios, et le build l'affiche pour chaque appareil choisi avant de demander s'il continue ; dans n, m demande l'usage de chaque interface au lieu d'un rôle.

Primero una clave SSH de administración, si aún no hay ninguna: las imágenes no aceptan otra cosa. Después las claves de radio, y después la construcción. OpenWrt 25.12 usa apk, no opkg.

En un terminal, ./build.sh abre un menú: los aparatos que ya están en profiles.txt, numerados. Se eligen varios por número o por papel (1 3, hedge), o Intro para todos. v cambia la versión de OpenWrt, w activa o quita la interfaz web, s escribe una imagen de Raspberry Pi en una tarjeta SD, n añade un aparato. Muestra la lista y pregunta antes de construir. Desde un script, ./build.sh all o un nombre de papel construye sin preguntas. Menú añadido el 2026-11-18 y probado con un Image Builder falso; aún no se ha lanzado con el de verdad. Comprobaciones añadidas el 2026-11-20, probadas de la misma manera: antes de descargar nada revisa cada línea elegida de profiles.txt (un papel conocido, una potencia de 0 a 30 dBm, las bandas que el papel necesita); una construcción reemplaza las imágenes de los aparatos que construye y guarda las demás en build/images; al final enumera lo que construyó y lo que saltó. Desde el 2026-11-21, cada línea del menú muestra lo que hacen sus puertos y radios, y la construcción lo muestra para cada aparato elegido antes de preguntar si sigue; en n, m pregunta el uso de cada interfaz en lugar de un papel.

Two checks run on every build, added on 2026-11-20. The signature check ran on the real Image Builder on 2026-11-21; the rule check too, on the Pi Zero 2 W feeder image: nothing forbidden in it. First, OpenWrt's signature. The checksums only prove that a download isn't broken, so the build also checks OpenWrt's signature on them, with OpenWrt's public keys kept in trust/, and prints the key that signed. A tampered server or mirror is then caught too. trust/ holds OpenWrt's two build keys, the 2016 one and the 2023 Nitrokey one (fingerprints on openwrt.org); on 2026-11-21, 25.12.6 was signed by the 2023 Nitrokey key (8A8BC12F46B836C0F9CDB36F1D53D1877742E911) and the check passed. The tool is gpgv, already there on Debian and Ubuntu. Second, the hard rules. Before building, the Image Builder lists every package the image would hold, and the build stops if it finds a DHCP or RA server, a firewall, PPP, or a web interface nobody asked for. Either check can be skipped when needed: CHECK_SIG=0 or CHECK_RULES=0 in front of ./build.sh, and the build says it skipped it. When OpenWrt signs with a new key, better check its fingerprint on openwrt.org's key page and add it to trust/ than skip the check.

Deux contrôles à chaque build, ajoutés le 2026-11-20. Le contrôle de signature a tourné avec le vrai Image Builder le 2026-11-21 ; le contrôle des règles aussi, sur l'image de mangeoire du Pi Zero 2 W : rien d'interdit dedans. D'abord la signature d'OpenWrt. Les sommes de contrôle prouvent seulement qu'un téléchargement n'est pas abîmé, alors le build vérifie aussi la signature d'OpenWrt dessus, avec les clés publiques d'OpenWrt gardées dans trust/, et affiche la clé qui a signé. Un serveur ou un miroir trafiqué est alors détecté lui aussi. trust/ contient les deux clés de build d'OpenWrt, celle de 2016 et celle de 2023 sur Nitrokey (empreintes sur openwrt.org) ; le 2026-11-21, la 25.12.6 était signée par la clé Nitrokey de 2023 (8A8BC12F46B836C0F9CDB36F1D53D1877742E911) et le contrôle est passé. L'outil est gpgv, déjà présent sur Debian et Ubuntu. Ensuite les règles dures. Avant de construire, l'Image Builder liste tous les paquets que l'image contiendrait, et le build s'arrête s'il y trouve un serveur DHCP ou RA, un pare-feu, PPP, ou une interface web que personne n'a demandée. Chaque contrôle peut être sauté si besoin : CHECK_SIG=0 ou CHECK_RULES=0 devant ./build.sh, et le build dit qu'il l'a sauté. Quand OpenWrt signe avec une nouvelle clé, mieux vaut vérifier son empreinte sur la page des clés d'openwrt.org et l'ajouter dans trust/ que sauter le contrôle.

Dos comprobaciones en cada construcción, añadidas el 2026-11-20. La comprobación de firma funcionó con el Image Builder de verdad el 2026-11-21; la de reglas también, con la imagen de comedero de la Pi Zero 2 W: nada prohibido en ella. Primero, la firma de OpenWrt. Las sumas de comprobación solo prueban que una descarga no está dañada, así que la construcción comprueba también la firma de OpenWrt sobre ellas, con las claves públicas de OpenWrt guardadas en trust/, y muestra la clave que firmó. Así se detecta también un servidor o una réplica manipulados. trust/ contiene las dos claves de construcción de OpenWrt, la de 2016 y la de 2023 en Nitrokey (huellas en openwrt.org); el 2026-11-21, la 25.12.6 estaba firmada por la clave Nitrokey de 2023 (8A8BC12F46B836C0F9CDB36F1D53D1877742E911) y la comprobación pasó. La herramienta es gpgv, que ya está en Debian y Ubuntu. Después, las reglas firmes. Antes de construir, el Image Builder enumera todos los paquetes que llevaría la imagen, y la construcción se para si encuentra un servidor DHCP o RA, un cortafuegos, PPP, o una interfaz web que nadie pidió. Cada comprobación se puede saltar si hace falta: CHECK_SIG=0 o CHECK_RULES=0 delante de ./build.sh, y la construcción dice que la saltó. Cuando OpenWrt firme con una clave nueva, mejor comprobar su huella en la página de claves de openwrt.org y añadirla a trust/ que saltar la comprobación.

Back up secrets/ offline, with your SSH key, for example on a USB stick kept away from the laptop. It holds the village's radio keys, and nothing else keeps a copy: not git, not the kit, not this handbook. If it is lost, a running perch or hedge still holds its key: cat /etc/sparrows/flyway.key (or hedge.key) over SSH. If every copy is lost, make a new key and reflash every perch, or every hedge.

Sauvegarder secrets/ hors ligne, avec la clé SSH, par exemple sur une clé USB rangée loin du portable. Il contient les clés radio du village, et rien d'autre n'en garde de copie : ni git, ni le kit, ni ce manuel. S'il est perdu, un perchoir ou une haie en service garde sa clé : cat /etc/sparrows/flyway.key (ou hedge.key) par SSH. Si toutes les copies sont perdues, il faut une nouvelle clé et reflasher tous les perchoirs, ou toutes les haies.

Guardar una copia de secrets/ fuera de línea, con la clave SSH, por ejemplo en una memoria USB lejos del portátil. Contiene las claves de radio del pueblo, y nada más guarda una copia: ni git, ni el kit, ni este manual. Si se pierde, una percha o un seto en servicio conserva su clave: cat /etc/sparrows/flyway.key (o hedge.key) por SSH. Si se pierden todas las copias, hace falta una clave nueva y volver a flashear todas las perchas, o todos los setos.

admin key
# Once per admin, on the admin laptop
ssh-keygen -t ed25519 -C "sparrows-admin"     # Enter = default path ~/.ssh/id_ed25519; set a passphrase
# Back up ~/.ssh/id_ed25519 (the private half) offline. The images lock the root password:
# without that file, the only way back into a station is failsafe mode at the unit.
cd sparrows-openwrt && mkdir -p keys secrets
shell
# Layout:  sparrows-openwrt/profiles.txt   role, target, device, power per band, web interface, [uses=]
#          sparrows-openwrt/uses.txt       what each port and radio does, per role
#          sparrows-openwrt/build.sh       ./build.sh [all|uses|<role>], ./build.sh sd
#          sparrows-openwrt/common/ station/   files for every unit: base, ports and radios
#          sparrows-openwrt/perch/ tree/ nest/ files only some units need (perch tool, Reticulum, nest)
#          sparrows-openwrt/ui/ devices/   web interface files, devices that need more than a line
#          sparrows-openwrt/trust/         OpenWrt's public keys, to check its signature
cd sparrows-openwrt
cp ~/.ssh/id_ed25519.pub keys/admin.pub                    # the build refuses to run without a key
printf '%s' 'the-flyway-passphrase' > secrets/flyway.key   # 5 GHz flyway (perch) only: same as the running flyway
head -c 32 /dev/urandom | base64 | tr -dc 'A-Za-z0-9' | head -c 32 > secrets/hedge.key   # 2.4 GHz flyway (hedge) only
# Back up secrets/ offline now, with your SSH key: no other copy of the radio keys exists
./build.sh                    # a menu: pick devices by number or role (Enter = all), n = add a device
./build.sh all                # no menu: every line of profiles.txt (also: perch, feeder, hedge, tree, nest)
./build.sh uses               # only show what each port and radio of every line will do
VER=25.12.x ./build.sh all    # another release (or v in the menu)
UI=1 ./build.sh all           # with the web interface where profiles.txt says ui (or w in the menu)
CHECK_SIG=0 ./build.sh all    # skip the check of OpenWrt's signature (new OpenWrt key? add it to trust/)
CHECK_RULES=0 ./build.sh all  # skip the check of each image's packages against the hard rules
ls build/images/latest/       # the images of the newest version (one folder per version, with SHA256SUMS)
profiles.txt
# Sparrows-Network: hardware that can take each role.
# One line per device and role. To support another device, add a line: nothing else changes.
#
# role = a role of uses.txt (perch, feeder, hedge, tree, nest), or several joined with +, e.g. tree+feeder:
#   what each port and radio does comes from uses.txt. The unit is named after it (tree-feeder-26de).
#
# tx2 / tx5 = power set at the radio, in dBm = legal EIRP limit (FR) - antenna gain.
#   2.4 GHz: 20 dBm EIRP.
#   5 GHz outdoor (ch 100-140): 30 dBm EIRP with TPC, 27 dBm without TPC.
# "-" = this band is not used on this device (its radio is switched off).
#
# ui = the web interface (LuCI) may go in this image, when the build asks for it (UI=1, or w in the menu).
# "-" = never: 8 MB of flash or 64 MB of RAM is too small (Sparrows Tao, Hard rules). No column = "-".
#
# uses = manual mode, for a special device: what each interface does, instead of a role's preset.
#   uses=<ports>,<2.4 GHz>,<5 GHz>,<services>   e.g. uses=wire,feeder,flyway,-
#   The role column is then only a name for the unit (letters, digits, -). Needs the ui column before it.
#   The menu writes these lines for you (n, then m). Uses are explained in uses.txt.
#
# profile = the OpenWrt profile name, or a folder in devices/ for a device that needs more than a
#   line (another OpenWrt profile, firmware to fetch, what its radios can do, its own first-boot
#   file). See devices/*/device.conf.
#
# role    target          profile                    tx2  tx5  ui  uses
perch     ath79/generic   tplink_cpe510-v3           -    17   -
feeder    ath79/generic   tplink_eap225-outdoor-v1   17   23   ui
feeder    ath79/generic   tplink_eap225-outdoor-v3   17   23   ui
hedge     ath79/generic   tplink_eap225-outdoor-v1   17   23   ui
hedge     ath79/generic   tplink_eap225-outdoor-v3   17   23   ui
# Pi Zero 2 W: 2.4 GHz only, with a USB Ethernet adapter for the village cable. Not yet tested.
feeder    bcm27xx/bcm2710 rpi-zero-2-w               17   -    ui
# Same Pi Zero 2 W as a tree: a Wi-Fi client of the village running Reticulum. No adapter needed.
tree      bcm27xx/bcm2710 rpi-zero-2-w               17   -    ui
# Both at once: its Wi-Fi serves Sparrows, Reticulum runs on the cable (USB adapter). Not yet tested.
tree+feeder bcm27xx/bcm2710 rpi-zero-2-w             17   -    ui
# Pi 4 as a nest, its own Wi-Fi as Sparrows Wi-Fi (2.4 GHz: its chip has no radar check for 5 GHz
# outdoor channels). Replaces the Debian nest once it passes the same checks. Not yet tested.
nest+feeder bcm27xx/bcm2711 rpi-4                    17   -    ui
build.sh
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Build the Sparrows-Network OpenWrt images with the OpenWrt Image Builder.
# One image per role and device: the same image goes on every unit of that role.
# What each port and radio does comes from the role (uses.txt), or from a manual line (profiles.txt).
# Runs on x86_64 Linux. Needs: wget, zstd, make, gcc, python3, perl, rsync (see OpenWrt IB docs), gpgv.
#
# Usage: ./build.sh              -> in a terminal: a menu to pick the devices (Enter = all of them)
#                                   without a terminal (a script, cron): every line of profiles.txt
#        ./build.sh all          -> every line of profiles.txt, no menu
#        ./build.sh perch        -> every line with that role, alone or combined (or: feeder, hedge,
#                                   tree, or a manual line's name), no menu
#        ./build.sh uses         -> what each port and radio of every line will do, nothing built
#        VER=25.12.x ./build.sh  -> another 25.12 release
#        UI=1 ./build.sh all     -> with the web interface (LuCI), only where profiles.txt says "ui"
#        ./build.sh sd [image]   -> write a Raspberry Pi image (.img.gz) to an SD card: asks which
#                                   reader, shows what is on it, and asks you to type its name first
# Versions: each build is stamped with the build tree's git version (git describe: the last tag, the
# commits since, the commit; "-dirty" when files are changed and not committed). The images go to
# build/images/<version>/, with a SHA256SUMS file; build/images/latest points to the newest. Older
# versions stay, to go back to. On a unit, /etc/sparrows/version and the login text say which
# version it runs, and on the admin laptop  git log <that version>..  shows what changed since.
#
# Two checks, each can be skipped when needed:
#   CHECK_SIG=0 ./build.sh     -> don't check OpenWrt's signature on the Image Builder's checksums
#                                 (its public keys are in trust/; a new key: check its fingerprint on
#                                 https://openwrt.org/docs/guide-user/security/signatures, add it there)
#   CHECK_RULES=0 ./build.sh   -> don't check the image's package list against the hard rules
#
# Downloads are kept and checked by their sha256, so each is fetched once: build/dl (OpenWrt's Image
# Builders and their signed checksums), build/firmware, build/wheels. Nothing there is deleted; remove a
# file by hand to fetch it again. An Image Builder that stops working is unpacked again from build/dl.
#
# The menu lists the devices already in profiles.txt with what their interfaces do (several can be
# picked at once), can change the release, can turn the web interface on or off, and can add a new
# device: it searches the Image Builder for the model, asks the role (or the uses of each interface,
# in manual mode), the radio power and whether the web interface fits, shows what each interface
# will do, and adds the line to profiles.txt marked "not yet tested".
# Web interface: LuCI, listening on the unit itself only (ui/files, reached through SSH). Never on a
# device marked "-" (8 MB flash / 64 MB RAM); there the image is built without it, and says so.
#
# Before the first build:
#   keys/*.pub           admin SSH public keys (root password is locked)
#   secrets/flyway.key   the 5 GHz flyway SAE passphrase (images with a 5 GHz flyway: perch)
#   secrets/hedge.key    the 2.4 GHz flyway SAE passphrase (images with a 2.4 GHz flyway: hedge)
#                        Each: one line of 8 to 63 printable ASCII characters, ideally letters/digits only.
#                        Generate one: head -c 32 /dev/urandom | base64 | tr -dc 'A-Za-z0-9' | head -c 32
#                        Images with a flyway contain its key: never publish them.
set -euo pipefail

VER="${VER:-25.12.6}"     # 2026-10-18, security fixes. Check https://downloads.openwrt.org/releases/ for the latest 25.12.x
UI="${UI:-0}"             # 1 = add the web interface where profiles.txt allows it
CHECK_SIG="${CHECK_SIG:-1}"
CHECK_RULES="${CHECK_RULES:-1}"
HERE="$(cd "$(dirname "$0")" && pwd)"
OUT="$HERE/build/images"
# The version of this build tree. Without git (a copy of the kit): "unversioned"
VERSION=$(git -C "$HERE" describe --tags --always --dirty 2>/dev/null || echo unversioned)
VERSION=${VERSION//\//-}
# The Image Builder's Makefiles break on a path with a space in it (seen 2026-11-21: "/home/me/00 - x"
# was cut at the first space, and "make info" failed). Then it works in a folder of its own, outside
# the tree; the images still land in build/images. SPARROWS_BUILD=<folder> chooses another one.
BUILD="${SPARROWS_BUILD:-$HERE/build}"
case "$BUILD" in *[[:space:]]*) BUILD="${XDG_CACHE_HOME:-$HOME/.cache}/sparrows-build" ;; esac

# Every image: no DHCP/RA server, no firewall, no PPP, no web UI (a station only bridges)
BASE="-dnsmasq -odhcpd-ipv6only -firewall4 -nftables -kmod-nft-offload -ppp -ppp-mod-pppoe -luci -luci-ssl"
# ...plus what its uses need
PKG_flyway="-wpad-basic-mbedtls wpad-mesh-mbedtls kmod-batman-adv batctl-default iw"
PKG_join="iw"
# A nest keeps odhcpd and dnsmasq (RA and DNS, no DHCP) and adds uhttpd: PKG_nest, in nest/role.conf.
# Reticulum: Python and the libraries Reticulum asks for; Reticulum and Nomad Network come as pinned
# wheels (tree/role.conf). Needs room: SD-card devices, not 8-16 MB flash.
PKG_reticulum="python3 python3-cryptography python3-pyserial iw"
# LuCI = luci-light minus its firewall and PPP pages. luci-light itself would bring firewall4 and
# nftables back (luci-app-firewall needs a firewall): no firewall is a hard rule. No HTTPS: it only
# listens on 127.0.0.1 and ::1
PKG_ui="uhttpd uhttpd-mod-ubus luci-mod-admin-full luci-theme-bootstrap luci-proto-ipv6 rpcd-mod-rrdns"
# Never in a station image (hard rules: only nests send RAs; no DHCP, firewall or PPP). CHECK_RULES
FORBIDDEN="dnsmasq dnsmasq-full dnsmasq-dhcpv6 odhcpd odhcpd-ipv6only firewall firewall4 nftables
nftables-json nftables-nojson iptables iptables-nft ppp ppp-mod-pppoe luci-app-firewall"
FORBIDDEN_NO_UI="luci-base uhttpd"     # ...and no web interface unless it was asked for

die() { echo "$*" >&2; exit 1; }

# The device lines of profiles.txt: role target profile tx2 tx5 [ui] [uses=...]. A # starts a comment anywhere
profiles() { awk '{ sub(/#.*/, "") } NF { $1 = $1; print }' "$HERE/profiles.txt"; }

# ---------------------------------------------------------------- uses of each interface

# The role presets of uses.txt: role ports 2.4GHz 5GHz services
presets() { awk '{ sub(/#.*/, "") } NF { $1 = $1; print }' "$HERE/uses.txt"; }
ROLES=$(presets | awk '{ printf "%s ", $1 }'); ROLES=${ROLES% }

has() { case " $1 " in *" $2 "*) return 0 ;; esac; return 1; }

# What a device's radios can do: CAN2 / CAN5 in devices/<profile>/device.conf, else everything
can() (
	CAN2='feeder flyway join' CAN5='feeder flyway join'
	if [ -f "$HERE/devices/$1/device.conf" ]; then
		# shellcheck source=/dev/null
		. "$HERE/devices/$1/device.conf"
	fi
	echo "$CAN2|$CAN5"
)

# The uses of a device line, as PORTS|USE2|USE5|RETICULUM|NEST (e.g. wire|flyway feeder|feeder|0|0),
# or why it can't be built (and returns 1). A role's preset is lenient (a band set to - is simply
# off); a manual uses= line is strict (it says exactly what it wants).
uses_of() {
	local R=$1 P=$2 X2=$3 X5=$4 SPEC=${5:-} PORTS='' U2='' U5='' S='' RNS=0 NEST=0 MANUAL=0
	local PART L A B C D W X C2 C5 N
	if [ -n "$SPEC" ]; then
		MANUAL=1
		IFS=, read -r PORTS U2 U5 S X <<<"${SPEC#uses=}"
		{ [ -n "$S" ] && [ -z "$X" ]; } || { echo "uses= needs 4 fields: ports,2.4GHz,5GHz,services"; return 1; }
		U2=${U2//+/ } U5=${U5//+/ }
		[ "$S" != - ] || S=''
		S=${S//+/ }
	else
		for PART in ${R//+/ }; do
			L=$(presets | awk -v r="$PART" '$1 == r')
			[ -n "$L" ] || { echo "unknown role '$PART' (roles: $ROLES)"; return 1; }
			read -r _ A B C D <<<"$L"
			[ "$A" != wire ] || PORTS=wire
			[ "$B" = off ] || U2="$U2 ${B//+/ }"
			[ "$C" = off ] || U5="$U5 ${C//+/ }"
			[ "$D" = - ] || S="$S $D"
		done
		[ -n "$PORTS" ] || PORTS=off
	fi
	case "$PORTS" in wire|off) ;; *) echo "ports '$PORTS' is not wire or off"; return 1 ;; esac
	for W in $U2 $U5; do
		case "$W" in feeder|flyway|join|off) ;; *) echo "'$W' is not a use (feeder, flyway, join, off)"; return 1 ;; esac
	done
	for W in $S; do
		case "$W" in
			reticulum) RNS=1 ;;
			nest) NEST=1 RNS=1 ;;      # a nest relays and stores for Reticulum too
			*) echo "'$W' is not a service (reticulum, nest, or -)"; return 1 ;;
		esac
	done
	# Each use once, always in the same order; off means nothing
	N=''; for W in flyway feeder join; do if has "$U2" "$W"; then N="$N $W"; fi; done; U2=${N# }
	N=''; for W in flyway feeder join; do if has "$U5" "$W"; then N="$N $W"; fi; done; U5=${N# }

	# A band set to - is off
	if [ "$X2" = - ] && [ -n "$U2" ]; then
		[ "$MANUAL" = 0 ] || { echo "uses on 2.4 GHz, but its power is -"; return 1; }
		U2=''
	fi
	if [ "$X5" = - ] && [ -n "$U5" ]; then
		[ "$MANUAL" = 0 ] || { echo "uses on 5 GHz, but its power is -"; return 1; }
		U5=''
	fi
	# What the chip can do (device.conf)
	IFS='|' read -r C2 C5 <<<"$(can "$P")"
	for W in $U2; do has "$C2" "$W" || { echo "this device's 2.4 GHz radio can't do '$W' (devices/$P/device.conf)"; return 1; }; done
	for W in $U5; do has "$C5" "$W" || { echo "this device's 5 GHz radio can't do '$W' (devices/$P/device.conf)"; return 1; }; done

	# A nest never joins: it serves the village, its radios don't hang on someone else's
	if [ "$NEST" = 1 ]; then U2=${U2% join}; U2=${U2%join}; U5=${U5% join}; U5=${U5%join}; fi
	# A radio that joins does nothing else: when it has another use, it doesn't join
	# (Reticulum then runs on the village bridge). And only one radio joins: the 2.4 GHz one
	if has "$U2" join && [ "$U2" != join ]; then U2=${U2% join}; fi
	if has "$U5" join && [ "$U5" != join ]; then U5=${U5% join}; fi
	if [ "$U2" = join ] && [ "$U5" = join ]; then U5=''; fi
	# One 5 GHz radio can't hold a flyway (ch 108) and a feeder (ch 132)
	if has "$U5" flyway && has "$U5" feeder; then
		echo "5 GHz: a flyway (ch 108) and a feeder (ch 132) can't share one radio"; return 1
	fi
	if [ -z "$U2$U5" ] && [ "$RNS" = 0 ]; then
		echo "with these bands, this unit would do nothing"; return 1
	fi
	echo "$PORTS|${U2:-off}|${U5:-off}|$RNS|$NEST"
}

# One line: "ports wire | 2.4 flyway+feeder | 5 feeder | Reticulum"
uses_short() {
	local PORTS U2 U5 RNS NEST
	IFS='|' read -r PORTS U2 U5 RNS NEST <<<"$1"
	printf 'ports %s | 2.4 %s | 5 %s%s' "$PORTS" "${U2// /+}" "${U5// /+}" \
		"$( if [ "$NEST" = 1 ]; then echo ' | nest'; elif [ "$RNS" = 1 ]; then echo ' | Reticulum'; fi )"
}

# What each interface will do, in words
uses_long() {
	local PORTS U2 U5 RNS NEST B U W T FIRST
	IFS='|' read -r PORTS U2 U5 RNS NEST <<<"$1"
	if [ "$PORTS" = wire ]; then
		printf '    %-10s %s\n' 'Ethernet' "wire: cable to the site's switch, part of the village (bridged)"
	else
		printf '    %-10s %s\n' 'Ethernet' "off: not in the village bridge"
	fi
	for B in 2.4 5; do
		if [ "$B" = 2.4 ]; then U=$U2; else U=$U5; fi
		if [ "$U" = off ]; then printf '    %-10s %s\n' "$B GHz" 'off'; continue; fi
		FIRST="$B GHz"
		for W in $U; do
			case "$W:$B" in
				feeder:*)  T='feeder: the open Sparrows Wi-Fi, for people'"'"'s devices' ;;
				flyway:5)  T='flyway: link to other perches, AP or client (perch ap), SAE, ch 108, batman-adv' ;;
				flyway:*)  T='flyway: mesh to the other units on ch 1 (802.11s, SAE), batman-adv' ;;
				join:*)    T='join: client of the Sparrows Wi-Fi, like a phone (not bridged)' ;;
			esac
			printf '    %-10s %s\n' "$FIRST" "$T"
			FIRST=''
		done
	done
	if [ "$RNS" = 1 ]; then
		if has "$U2 $U5" join; then W='wsta, the Wi-Fi client'; else W='br-lan, the village bridge'; fi
		printf '    %-10s %s\n' 'Services' "Reticulum relay + the Sparrows page, on $W"
	fi
	if [ "$NEST" = 1 ]; then
		printf '    %-10s %s\n' '' 'nest: router advertisements, DNS (sparrows.lan, catch-all), the welcome page,'
		printf '    %-10s %s\n' '' '      the message store (lxmd), on a fixed village address from the MAC'
	fi
}

# Why a device line can't be built (nothing if it can): caught here, not on a mast
bad_line() {
	local R=$1 T=${2:-} P=${3:-} X2=${4:-} X5=${5:-} U=${6:--} SPEC=${7:-} REST=${8:-} X MAP
	if [ -n "$SPEC" ]; then
		[[ "$SPEC" =~ ^uses= ]] || { echo "7th column '$SPEC' is not uses=..."; return; }
		[[ "$R" =~ ^[a-z][a-z0-9-]*$ ]] || { echo "name '$R' (manual line): letters, digits and - only"; return; }
	else
		[[ "$R" =~ ^[a-z][a-z0-9+]*$ ]] || { echo "role '$R' is not a role or roles joined with +"; return; }
	fi
	[[ "$T" =~ ^[a-z0-9_-]+/[a-z0-9_-]+$ ]] || { echo "target '$T' is not name/subtarget"; return; }
	[ -n "$P" ] || { echo "no profile"; return; }
	for X in "$X2" "$X5"; do
		[[ "$X" =~ ^(-|[0-9]|[12][0-9]|30)$ ]] || { echo "radio power '$X' is not - or 0 to 30 dBm"; return; }
	done
	[[ "$U" =~ ^(ui|-)$ ]] || { echo "6th column '$U' is not ui or -"; return; }
	[ -z "$REST" ] || { echo "too many columns"; return; }
	MAP=$(uses_of "$R" "$P" "$X2" "$X5" "$SPEC") || echo "$MAP"
}

ib_name() { echo "openwrt-imagebuilder-${VER}-${1/\//-}.Linux-x86_64"; }

# OpenWrt's signature on a file, checked with the public keys kept in trust/. The checksums only prove
# the download isn't broken; the signature proves it is OpenWrt's, whatever server or mirror it came
# from. gpgv is enough: python unpacks the keys, so GnuPG itself isn't needed. Prints who signed.
check_sig() {
	local FILE=$1 SIG=$2 KR="$BUILD/trust.gpg" GH="$BUILD/gnupg" STATUS ID
	command -v gpgv >/dev/null || { echo "gpgv is missing (Debian/Ubuntu: apt install gpgv), or CHECK_SIG=0 to skip this check" >&2; return 1; }
	compgen -G "$HERE/trust/*.asc" >/dev/null || { echo "No OpenWrt public key in trust/*.asc, or CHECK_SIG=0 to skip this check" >&2; return 1; }
	mkdir -p "$GH" && chmod 700 "$GH"
	python3 -I -c '
import base64, sys
out = open(sys.argv[1], "wb")              # armored key files -> one binary keyring, as gpgv reads it
for name in sys.argv[2:]:
    body, inside, header = [], False, False
    for line in open(name, encoding="ascii", errors="replace"):
        line = line.strip()
        if line.startswith("-----BEGIN PGP PUBLIC KEY BLOCK"):
            inside, header, body = True, True, []
        elif line.startswith("-----END PGP PUBLIC KEY BLOCK"):
            out.write(base64.b64decode("".join(body)))
            inside = False
        elif inside and header:
            header = line != ""            # the armor headers end at the first empty line
        elif inside and not line.startswith("="):
            body.append(line)
' "$KR" "$HERE"/trust/*.asc || return 1
	if STATUS=$(gpgv --homedir "$GH" --status-fd 1 --keyring "$KR" "$SIG" "$FILE" 2>/dev/null); then
		echo "$(basename "$FILE"): signed by OpenWrt key $(printf '%s\n' "$STATUS" | awk '$2 == "VALIDSIG" { print $NF }')" >&2
		return 0
	fi
	ID=$(printf '%s\n' "$STATUS" | awk '$2 == "NO_PUBKEY" { print $3 }')
	if [ -n "$ID" ]; then
		echo "$(basename "$FILE"): signed by key $ID, which is not in trust/. If openwrt.org lists it" >&2
		echo "  (https://openwrt.org/docs/guide-user/security/signatures), add it to trust/; or CHECK_SIG=0 to skip" >&2
	else
		echo "$(basename "$FILE"): signature check FAILED. Don't use this download (CHECK_SIG=0 skips the check)" >&2
	fi
	return 1
}

# A file checked by its sha256 and kept: fetched only when it is missing or doesn't match. A download
# goes to .part first, so a cut-off one never passes for the real file. A progress bar in a terminal
fetch() {
	local URL=$1 FILE=$2 SUM=$3 PROGRESS=''
	if echo "$SUM  $FILE" | sha256sum -c --status - 2>/dev/null; then return 0; fi
	mkdir -p "$(dirname "$FILE")"
	echo "Fetching ${FILE##*/}..." >&2
	[ ! -t 2 ] || PROGRESS='--show-progress'
	wget -q $PROGRESS -O "$FILE.part" "$URL" || { rm -f "$FILE.part"; echo "Download failed: $URL" >&2; return 1; }
	echo "$SUM  $FILE.part" | sha256sum -c --status - || { rm -f "$FILE.part"; echo "${FILE##*/}: checksum FAILED" >&2; return 1; }
	mv "$FILE.part" "$FILE"
}

# Image Builder for a target. What OpenWrt publishes is kept in build/dl: its signed checksums and the
# Image Builder itself. Unpacked again from there when needed (FRESH=1, or the folder is gone);
# downloaded again only when the kept file is missing or doesn't match the checksums. Runs in a subshell.
get_ib() (
	TARGET=$1 FRESH=${2:-0}
	IB=$(ib_name "$TARGET")
	URL="https://downloads.openwrt.org/releases/${VER}/targets/${TARGET}"
	D="$BUILD/dl"
	mkdir -p "$D" && cd "$BUILD" || exit 1
	[ "$FRESH" = 0 ] || rm -rf "$IB"
	[ -d "$IB" ] && exit 0
	# OpenWrt's checksums for this release and target, with their signature: fetched once, checked every time
	if [ ! -s "$D/$IB.sha256sums" ]; then
		wget -q -O "$D/$IB.sha256sums.part" "$URL/sha256sums" && mv "$D/$IB.sha256sums.part" "$D/$IB.sha256sums" || \
			{ rm -f "$D/$IB.sha256sums.part"; echo "No $VER release for $TARGET" >&2; exit 1; }
		rm -f "$D/$IB.sha256sums.asc"
	fi
	if [ "$CHECK_SIG" = 1 ]; then
		[ -s "$D/$IB.sha256sums.asc" ] || wget -q -O "$D/$IB.sha256sums.asc" "$URL/sha256sums.asc" || \
			{ rm -f "$D/$IB.sha256sums.asc"; echo "No signature at $URL/sha256sums.asc (CHECK_SIG=0 skips this check)" >&2; exit 1; }
		check_sig "$D/$IB.sha256sums" "$D/$IB.sha256sums.asc" || exit 1
	fi
	SUM=$(awk -v f="$IB.tar.zst" '$2 == f || $2 == "*" f { print $1 }' "$D/$IB.sha256sums")
	[ -n "$SUM" ] || { echo "$IB.tar.zst is not in OpenWrt's checksums for $TARGET" >&2; exit 1; }
	fetch "$URL/$IB.tar.zst" "$D/$IB.tar.zst" "$SUM" || exit 1
	# Unpacked aside, then moved: a cut-off unpack is never taken for a complete Image Builder
	echo "Unpacking the Image Builder for $TARGET ($VER)..." >&2
	rm -rf "$IB.part" && mkdir "$IB.part"
	tar -C "$IB.part" --zstd -xf "$D/$IB.tar.zst" && mv "$IB.part/$IB" "$IB" && rmdir "$IB.part" || exit 1
)

# A device left out of this build: said now, and again in the summary at the end
skip() { echo "$*" >&2; echo "$*" >> "$BUILD/.skipped"; exit 0; }

# Build one image. Runs in a subshell: "exit 0" skips this device, any error stops the build.
build_one() (
	ROLE=$1 TARGET=$2 PROFILE=$3 TX2=$4 TX5=$5 UIOK=${6:--} SPEC=${7:-}
	MAP=$(uses_of "$ROLE" "$PROFILE" "$TX2" "$TX5" "$SPEC") || die "$ROLE $PROFILE: $MAP"
	IFS='|' read -r PORTS USE2 USE5 RNS NEST <<<"$MAP"
	[ "$USE2" != off ] || USE2=''
	[ "$USE5" != off ] || USE5=''
	NAMEROLE=${ROLE//+/-}            # tree+feeder -> tree-feeder: in the unit's name and the image's
	WITH_UI=0 NAME="sparrows-$NAMEROLE"

	# --- A device that needs more than a line: devices/<name>/device.conf (see profiles.txt) ---
	IB_PROFILE=$PROFILE DEV_PACKAGES='' MACDEV='' CH2='' FW_URL='' FW_DIR='' FW_FILES='' ROOTFS_MB=''
	DEV="$HERE/devices/$PROFILE"
	if [ -f "$DEV/device.conf" ]; then
		# shellcheck source=/dev/null
		. "$DEV/device.conf"
	else
		DEV=''
	fi
	if [ "$UI" = 1 ]; then
		if [ "$UIOK" = ui ]; then
			WITH_UI=1 NAME="sparrows-$NAMEROLE-ui"
		else
			echo "$ROLE $PROFILE: too small for the web interface (profiles.txt), built without it" >&2
		fi
	fi

	# --- SAE passphrase for each flyway: 5 GHz (perch) and 2.4 GHz (hedge) ---
	KEYS=''
	if has "$USE5" flyway; then KEYS="$KEYS flyway.key"; fi
	if has "$USE2" flyway; then KEYS="$KEYS hedge.key"; fi
	for KEYF in $KEYS; do
		[ -s "$HERE/secrets/$KEYF" ] || die "$ROLE $PROFILE: put the SAE passphrase in secrets/$KEYF first"
		# Seen 2026-11-14: a key outside 8-63 printable ASCII never links (see Sparrows Tao, Pitfalls).
		# The whole file is checked, as the unit reads it: one line (a final newline is fine)
		( LC_ALL=C; K=$(<"$HERE/secrets/$KEYF"); RE='^[ -~]{8,63}$'; [[ $K =~ $RE ]] ) || \
			die "secrets/$KEYF must be one line of 8 to 63 printable ASCII characters (no accents)"
	done

	# --- Files for this unit: common base, the uses, keys, what the uses need, radio limits ---
	F="$BUILD/files-$NAMEROLE-$PROFILE"
	rm -rf "$F"
	cp -a "$HERE/common/files" "$F"
	cp -a "$HERE/station/files/." "$F/"
	if has "$USE5" flyway; then cp -a "$HERE/perch/files/." "$F/"; fi      # the perch tool
	if [ "$RNS" = 1 ]; then cp -a "$HERE/tree/files/." "$F/"; fi          # Reticulum + the page
	if [ "$NEST" = 1 ]; then cp -a "$HERE/nest/files/." "$F/"; fi         # RA, DNS, welcome page, lxmd
	install -d -m 700 "$F/etc/dropbear"
	cat "$HERE"/keys/*.pub > "$F/etc/dropbear/authorized_keys"
	chmod 600 "$F/etc/dropbear/authorized_keys"
	install -d -m 755 "$F/etc/sparrows"
	printf "ROLE='%s'\nTX2='%s'\nTX5='%s'\nPORTS='%s'\nUSE2='%s'\nUSE5='%s'\nRETICULUM='%s'\nNEST='%s'\n" \
		"$NAMEROLE" "$TX2" "$TX5" "$PORTS" "$USE2" "$USE5" "$RNS" "$NEST" > "$F/etc/sparrows/station"
	printf "SPARROWS_VERSION='%s'\nOPENWRT='%s'\nBUILT='%s'\nLINE='%s'\n" "$VERSION" "$VER" "$(date -u +%F)" \
		"$ROLE $TARGET $PROFILE $TX2 $TX5 $UIOK${SPEC:+ $SPEC}" > "$F/etc/sparrows/version"
	[ -z "$MACDEV" ] || printf "MACDEV='%s'\n" "$MACDEV" >> "$F/etc/sparrows/station"
	[ -z "$CH2" ] || printf "CH2='%s'\n" "$CH2" >> "$F/etc/sparrows/station"
	if [ -n "$DEV" ] && [ -d "$DEV/files" ]; then
		cp -a "$DEV/files/." "$F/"
	fi
	# Firmware the device needs and OpenWrt doesn't ship: fetched once, checked file by file
	if [ -n "$FW_FILES" ]; then
		C="$BUILD/firmware/$PROFILE"
		mkdir -p "$C" "$F/$FW_DIR"
		while read -r SUM FILE; do
			[ -n "$FILE" ] || continue
			fetch "$FW_URL/$FILE" "$C/$FILE" "$SUM" || die "$ROLE $PROFILE: firmware $FILE"
			install -m 644 "$C/$FILE" "$F/$FW_DIR/$FILE"
		done <<<"$FW_FILES"
	fi
	for KEYF in $KEYS; do
		install -m 600 "$HERE/secrets/$KEYF" "$F/etc/sparrows/$KEYF"
	done
	if [ "$WITH_UI" = 1 ]; then
		cp -a "$HERE/ui/files/." "$F/"
	fi
	chmod 755 "$F"/etc/uci-defaults/*
	for D in usr/sbin usr/bin etc/init.d; do
		if [ -d "$F/$D" ]; then chmod 755 "$F/$D"/*; fi
	done

	# --- Reticulum: Python wheels (tree/role.conf), fetched once, checked, unpacked into the image ---
	WHEELS='' HANDBOOK_TO='' PKG_nest=''
	if [ "$RNS" = 1 ]; then
		# shellcheck source=/dev/null
		. "$HERE/tree/role.conf"
	fi
	if [ "$NEST" = 1 ]; then
		# shellcheck source=/dev/null
		. "$HERE/nest/role.conf"
	fi
	if [ -n "$WHEELS" ]; then
		C="$BUILD/wheels"
		mkdir -p "$C" "$F/usr/lib/sparrows/python"
		while read -r SUM URL; do
			[ -n "$URL" ] || continue
			W="$C/${URL##*/}"
			fetch "$URL" "$W" "$SUM" || die "$ROLE $PROFILE: Python wheel ${URL##*/}"
			python3 -I -m zipfile -e "$W" "$F/usr/lib/sparrows/python/"
		done <<<"$WHEELS"
	fi
	# The built handbook: offered by the page node, and the welcome page of a nest
	if [ -n "$HANDBOOK_TO" ]; then
		if [ -f "$HERE/../tao/index.html" ]; then
			for H in $HANDBOOK_TO; do install -D -m 644 "$HERE/../tao/index.html" "$F/$H"; done
		else
			echo "$ROLE $PROFILE: no ../tao/index.html next to the build tree, so no handbook file in the image" >&2
		fi
	fi

	get_ib "$TARGET" || die "No Image Builder for $TARGET"
	cd "$BUILD/$(ib_name "$TARGET")"
	# The profile list. An Image Builder that doesn't run (a cut-off unpack, a moved folder) is unpacked
	# once more from the kept download, then said as such: never taken for a missing profile
	if ! INFO=$(make info 2>&1); then
		echo "$ROLE $PROFILE: the Image Builder doesn't run, unpacking it again from build/dl..." >&2
		cd "$BUILD" && get_ib "$TARGET" 1 && cd "$BUILD/$(ib_name "$TARGET")" || die "No Image Builder for $TARGET"
		INFO=$(make info 2>&1) || die "$ROLE $PROFILE: the Image Builder doesn't run (make info):"$'\n'"$(printf '%s\n' "$INFO" | tail -n 4)"
	fi
	printf '%s\n' "$INFO" | grep -q "^$IB_PROFILE:" || skip "$ROLE $PROFILE: no profile $IB_PROFILE in OpenWrt $VER $TARGET"
	# The packages: the base, then what the uses need, each package once
	PACKAGES="$BASE"
	if [ "$NEST" = 1 ]; then PACKAGES=$(printf '%s\n' $BASE | grep -v -x -e -dnsmasq -e -odhcpd-ipv6only | tr '\n' ' '); fi
	if has "$USE2 $USE5" flyway; then PACKAGES="$PACKAGES $PKG_flyway"; fi
	if has "$USE2 $USE5" join; then PACKAGES="$PACKAGES $PKG_join"; fi
	if [ "$RNS" = 1 ]; then PACKAGES="$PACKAGES $PKG_reticulum"; fi
	PACKAGES="$PACKAGES $PKG_nest $DEV_PACKAGES"
	[ "$WITH_UI" = 0 ] || PACKAGES="$PACKAGES $PKG_ui"
	PACKAGES=$(printf '%s\n' $PACKAGES | awk '!seen[$0]++' | tr '\n' ' ')
	[ -z "$DEV" ] || NAME="$NAME-$PROFILE"       # e.g. sparrows-feeder-rpi-zero-2-w-...-rpi-3-...
	# Image names: openwrt-<release>-<NAME>-<target>-<profile>-squashfs-factory.bin. Matched exactly, so
	# sparrows-feeder never picks up sparrows-feeder-ui, and no image of an earlier run passes for this one
	STEM="$NAME-${TARGET/\//-}-$IB_PROFILE"
	rm -f bin/targets/"$TARGET"/*-"$STEM"-*
	# The hard rules, checked by the machine: the Image Builder lists every package the image would
	# hold, dependencies included, before building it. Seen 2026-11-20: luci-light brought a firewall back
	if [ "$CHECK_RULES" = 1 ]; then
		MANIFEST=$(make manifest PROFILE="$IB_PROFILE" PACKAGES="$PACKAGES" STRIP_ABI=1) || \
			die "$ROLE $PROFILE: the Image Builder can't put these packages together (make manifest)"
		NO="$FORBIDDEN"; [ "$WITH_UI" = 1 ] || NO="$NO $FORBIDDEN_NO_UI"
		# A nest is the one unit that sends RAs and answers DNS (no DHCP), and serves the page.
		# Still no firewall, no PPP, no DHCPv6 server
		if [ "$NEST" = 1 ]; then NO=$(printf '%s\n' $NO | grep -v -x -e dnsmasq -e odhcpd-ipv6only -e uhttpd | tr '\n' ' '); fi
		BAD=$(printf '%s\n' "$MANIFEST" | awk -v no="$NO" 'BEGIN { split(no, a); for (i in a) f[a[i]] = 1 }
			($1 in f) { printf "%s ", $1 }')
		[ -z "$BAD" ] || die "$ROLE $PROFILE: the image would hold ${BAD}against the hard rules (no DHCP, RA, firewall or PPP on a station; no web interface unless asked). Fix the package lists in build.sh, or CHECK_RULES=0 to build it anyway"
	fi
	# SD-card devices: the root partition's size, which holds the system and, after it, the space where
	# the unit keeps its changes and data. OpenWrt's default is small (104 MB on a Pi): device.conf can
	# ask for more with ROOTFS_MB. The rest of the card stays unused either way
	SIZE=()
	[ -z "$ROOTFS_MB" ] || SIZE=(ROOTFS_PARTSIZE="$ROOTFS_MB")
	if ! make image PROFILE="$IB_PROFILE" PACKAGES="$PACKAGES" FILES="$F" EXTRA_IMAGE_NAME="$NAME" "${SIZE[@]}"; then
		# With the web interface, too big for the flash is the usual reason: skip it, build the others
		[ "$WITH_UI" = 0 ] || skip "$ROLE $PROFILE: no image with the web interface (too big?): build it without, or set ui to - in profiles.txt"
		die "$ROLE $PROFILE: make image failed (its messages are above)"
	fi
	# .bin for most routers, .img.gz for SD-card devices such as the Raspberry Pi. squashfs only: a Pi
	# build also makes ext4 images, but going back to the image's defaults (firstboot) needs squashfs.
	# No initramfs image either: it runs from RAM only, nothing to flash
	shopt -s nullglob
	IMGS=()
	for I in bin/targets/"$TARGET"/*-"$STEM"-*{.bin,.img.gz}; do
		case "$I" in *-ext4-*|*-initramfs-*) ;; *) IMGS+=("$I") ;; esac
	done
	[ "${#IMGS[@]}" -gt 0 ] || die "$ROLE $PROFILE: make image gave no .bin or .img.gz"
	# They replace this device's earlier images (any release, with or without -ui). Other devices' stay
	# Into this version's folder. A rebuild of the same version replaces this device's images there
	V="$OUT/$VERSION"
	mkdir -p "$V"
	rm -f "$V"/*-"sparrows-$NAMEROLE"{,-ui}"${DEV:+-$PROFILE}-${TARGET/\//-}-$IB_PROFILE"-*
	cp "${IMGS[@]}" "$V/"
	printf '%s\n' "${IMGS[@]##*/}" >> "$BUILD/.built"
)

# ---------------------------------------------------------------- menu (terminal only)

ask() { local a; read -r -p "$1" a </dev/tty; printf '%s' "$a"; }

# Power at the radio: "-" (band off) or a whole number of dBm, 0 to 30 (no leading zero: 08 is octal)
ask_tx() {
	local v
	while :; do
		v=$(ask "$1")
		[[ "$v" =~ ^(-|[0-9]|[12][0-9]|30)$ ]] && { printf '%s' "$v"; return; }
		echo "  A number of dBm (0 to 30), or - if this band is not used." >&2
	done
}

# Manual mode: the use of each interface, asked one by one. Prints uses=ports,2.4,5,services
ask_uses() {
	local P U2 U5 S
	echo "Manual mode. Uses: wire, feeder (open Wi-Fi), flyway (link carrying batman-adv)," >&2
	echo "join (client of the Sparrows Wi-Fi), off. Several on one radio with +, e.g. flyway+feeder." >&2
	echo "Services: nest (RAs, DNS, welcome page, message store, Reticulum), reticulum, or none." >&2
	P=$(ask "Ethernet ports (wire or off): ")
	U2=$(ask "2.4 GHz radio (e.g. feeder, flyway+feeder, join, off): ")
	U5=$(ask "5 GHz radio (e.g. feeder, flyway, join, off): ")
	S=$(ask "Services (nest, reticulum, or Enter for none; both need an SD-card device): ")
	printf 'uses=%s,%s,%s,%s' "${P:-wire}" "${U2:-off}" "${U5:-off}" "${S:--}"
}
