# Find a device in the Image Builder of its target, then add it to profiles.txt
add_device() {
	local TARGET WORD FOUND N PROFILE R RS TX2 TX5 UIOK LINE WHY NEW='' SPEC='' MAP i
	echo
	echo "The target is on the device's page of the OpenWrt Table of Hardware, e.g. ath79/generic, bcm27xx/bcm2711."
	TARGET=$(ask "Target: ")
	[[ "$TARGET" =~ ^[a-z0-9_-]+/[a-z0-9_-]+$ ]] || { echo "Not a target (form: name/subtarget)."; return; }
	get_ib "$TARGET" || return 0
	WORD=$(ask "Part of the model name, e.g. cpe510, eap225, rpi-4: ")
	mapfile -t FOUND < <(cd "$BUILD/$(ib_name "$TARGET")" && make info 2>/dev/null \
		| sed -n 's/^\([A-Za-z0-9_.,-]*\):$/\1/p' | grep -i -F -- "$WORD" || true)
	[ "${#FOUND[@]}" -gt 0 ] || { echo "No model matching '$WORD' in $TARGET ($VER)."; return; }
	for i in "${!FOUND[@]}"; do printf '  %2d) %s\n' $((i + 1)) "${FOUND[$i]}"; done
	N=$(ask "Which one? ")
	[[ "$N" =~ ^[0-9]+$ && "$N" -ge 1 && "$N" -le "${#FOUND[@]}" ]] || { echo "Nothing added."; return; }
	PROFILE=${FOUND[$((N - 1))]}

	echo "Roles: $ROLES. Join roles with + to have both on one unit (e.g. tree+feeder)."
	RS=$(ask "Role(s) for this device (several with spaces), or m for manual mode: ")
	[ -n "$RS" ] || { echo "Nothing added."; return; }
	if [ "$RS" = m ]; then
		RS=$(ask "A name for this kind of unit (letters, digits, -), e.g. garden: ")
		[[ "$RS" =~ ^[a-z][a-z0-9-]*$ ]] || { echo "Not a name. Nothing added."; return; }
		SPEC=$(ask_uses)
	fi

	echo "Power to set at the radio = legal EIRP (FR) - antenna gain. 2.4 GHz: 20 dBm EIRP."
	echo "5 GHz outdoor (ch 100-140): 30 dBm EIRP with TPC, 27 dBm without. - = band not used."
	TX2=$(ask_tx "2.4 GHz power in dBm, or -: ")
	TX5=$(ask_tx "5 GHz power in dBm, or -: ")
	case "$(ask "At least 16 MB of flash and 128 MB of RAM (web interface allowed)? [y/N] ")" in
		[yY]*) UIOK=ui ;; *) UIOK=- ;;
	esac

	for R in $RS; do
		WHY=$(bad_line "$R" "$TARGET" "$PROFILE" "$TX2" "$TX5" "$UIOK" "$SPEC")
		if [ -n "$WHY" ]; then
			echo "  $R not added: $WHY."
			continue
		fi
		if profiles | awk -v r="$R" -v p="$PROFILE" '$1 == r && $3 == p { f = 1 } END { exit !f }'; then
			echo "  $R $PROFILE is already in profiles.txt: left as it is."
			continue
		fi
		MAP=$(uses_of "$R" "$PROFILE" "$TX2" "$TX5" "$SPEC")
		echo "  $R on $PROFILE:"
		uses_long "$MAP"
		LINE=$(printf '%-9s %-15s %-26s %-4s %-4s %-3s %s' "$R" "$TARGET" "$PROFILE" "$TX2" "$TX5" "$UIOK" "$SPEC")
		NEW="$NEW${LINE% }"$'\n'
	done
	[ -n "$NEW" ] || return 0
	printf '\nTo add to profiles.txt:\n%s' "$NEW"
	case "$(ask "Add? [Y/n] ")" in [nN]*) echo "Nothing added."; return ;; esac
	[ -z "$(tail -c 1 "$HERE/profiles.txt")" ] || echo >> "$HERE/profiles.txt"   # last line not cut short
	{ printf '# added %s from the build menu: not yet tested\n' "$(date +%F)"; printf '%s' "$NEW"; } >> "$HERE/profiles.txt"
	echo "Added. It is now in the list."
}

# The lines whose role is R, alone or joined with +, or the manual line named R
lines_of_role() { awk -v r="$1" '{ n = split($1, p, "+"); for (i = 1; i <= n; i++) if (p[i] == r) { print; next } }'; }

# Sets CHOSEN to the picked device lines (and VER, if the release was changed)
menu() {
	local LINES CH T i R P X2 X5 U S PICK OK MAP
	while :; do
		mapfile -t LINES < <(profiles)
		echo
		echo "Sparrows images, OpenWrt $VER, web interface $( [ "$UI" = 1 ] && echo ON || echo off ). Devices in profiles.txt:"
		for i in "${!LINES[@]}"; do
			read -r R _ P X2 X5 U S <<<"${LINES[$i]}"
			[ "${U:--}" = ui ] && U=' +web' || U=''
			if MAP=$(uses_of "$R" "$P" "$X2" "$X5" "${S:-}"); then MAP=$(uses_short "$MAP"); else MAP="CAN'T BUILD: $MAP"; fi
			printf '  %2d) %-11s %-26s %s%s\n' $((i + 1)) "$R" "$P" "$MAP" "$U"
		done
		echo "   n) add a device that is not in the list (m there: manual mode, the use of each interface)"
		echo "   v) another OpenWrt release"
		echo "   w) web interface on/off (only where it fits)"
		echo "   s) write a Raspberry Pi image to an SD card (no build)"
		echo "   q) quit"
		CH=$(ask "Build: numbers and/or roles, with spaces (e.g. 1 3, or hedge). Enter = all: ")
		case "$CH" in
			''|all) CHOSEN=$(printf '%s\n' "${LINES[@]}"); return ;;
			q) exit 0 ;;
			w) if [ "$UI" = 1 ]; then UI=0; else UI=1; fi; continue ;;
			s) write_sd; continue ;;
			n) add_device; continue ;;
			v) T=$(ask "Release (now $VER), e.g. 25.12.6: ")
			   if [[ "$T" =~ ^[0-9]+\.[0-9]+\.[0-9]+(-rc[0-9]+)?$ ]]; then VER=$T; else echo "Not a release number."; fi
			   continue ;;
		esac
		PICK=() OK=1
		for T in $CH; do
			if [[ "$T" =~ ^[0-9]+$ ]] && [ "$T" -ge 1 ] && [ "$T" -le "${#LINES[@]}" ]; then
				PICK+=("${LINES[$((T - 1))]}")
			elif S=$(printf '%s\n' "${LINES[@]}" | lines_of_role "$T") && [ -n "$S" ]; then
				mapfile -t -O "${#PICK[@]}" PICK <<<"$S"
			else
				echo "'$T' is not in the list."; OK=0
			fi
		done
		if [ "$OK" = 1 ] && [ "${#PICK[@]}" -gt 0 ]; then
			CHOSEN=$(printf '%s\n' "${PICK[@]}" | awk '!seen[$0]++')     # each device once, even if picked twice
			return
		fi
	done
}

# ---------------------------------------------------------------- SD card (Raspberry Pi)

# Disks that can be a card reader: removable, USB or MMC. Never a disk that holds /, /boot, /home,
# /usr, /var or swap. Prints: device <tab> size <tab> model
sd_disks() {
	lsblk -Jpo NAME,TYPE,RM,TRAN,SIZE,MODEL,MOUNTPOINT | python3 -c '
import json, sys
SYSTEM = {"/", "/boot", "/boot/efi", "/boot/firmware", "/home", "/usr", "/var", "[SWAP]"}
def mounts(d):
    m = {d.get("mountpoint")}
    for c in d.get("children") or []:
        m |= mounts(c)
    return m
for d in json.load(sys.stdin)["blockdevices"]:
    if d.get("type") != "disk" or mounts(d) & SYSTEM:
        continue
    if d.get("rm") in (True, 1, "1") or d.get("tran") == "usb" or "mmcblk" in d["name"]:
        print(d["name"], d.get("size") or "?", (d.get("model") or "").strip() or "-", sep="\t")
'
}

# Write an SD-card image (.img.gz) to a card. Asks for the image, the reader, then the disk name.
write_sd() {
	local IMGS DISKS N IMG DEV SIZE MODEL BYTES P i
	[ -t 0 ] || die "Writing an SD card needs a terminal: it asks before erasing anything."
	if [ -n "${1:-}" ]; then
		IMGS=("$1")
	else
		shopt -s nullglob
		IMGS=("$OUT"/latest/*factory*.img.gz)
		[ "${#IMGS[@]}" -gt 0 ] || IMGS=("$OUT"/*/*factory*.img.gz "$OUT"/*.img.gz)
		shopt -u nullglob
	fi
	[ "${#IMGS[@]}" -gt 0 ] || { echo "No .img.gz in $OUT/latest: build a Raspberry Pi image first."; return 0; }
	IMG=${IMGS[0]}
	if [ "${#IMGS[@]}" -gt 1 ]; then
		for i in "${!IMGS[@]}"; do printf '  %2d) %s\n' $((i + 1)) "${IMGS[$i]#"$OUT"/}"; done
		N=$(ask "Which image? ")
		[[ "$N" =~ ^[0-9]+$ && "$N" -ge 1 && "$N" -le "${#IMGS[@]}" ]] || { echo "Cancelled, nothing written."; return 0; }
		IMG=${IMGS[$((N - 1))]}
	fi
	[ -s "$IMG" ] || die "No such image: $IMG"

	ask "Put the SD card in its reader, then press Enter. " >/dev/null
	mapfile -t DISKS < <(sd_disks)
	[ "${#DISKS[@]}" -gt 0 ] || { echo "No card reader found (removable, USB or MMC). Check with: lsblk"; return 0; }
	echo "Card readers and USB disks:"
	for i in "${!DISKS[@]}"; do
		IFS=$'\t' read -r DEV SIZE MODEL <<<"${DISKS[$i]}"
		printf '  %2d) %-14s %8s  %s\n' $((i + 1)) "$DEV" "$SIZE" "$MODEL"
	done
	N=$(ask "Which one holds the card? (Enter = cancel): ")
	[[ "$N" =~ ^[0-9]+$ && "$N" -ge 1 && "$N" -le "${#DISKS[@]}" ]] || { echo "Cancelled, nothing written."; return 0; }
	IFS=$'\t' read -r DEV SIZE MODEL <<<"${DISKS[$((N - 1))]}"

	echo
	echo "EVERYTHING on $DEV ($SIZE, $MODEL) will be erased:"
	lsblk -po NAME,SIZE,FSTYPE,LABEL,MOUNTPOINT "$DEV"
	echo "and replaced by: $(basename "$IMG")"
	[ "$(ask "To go on, type the disk name (${DEV#/dev/}): ")" = "${DEV#/dev/}" ] || { echo "Cancelled, nothing written."; return 0; }

	# Unpacked on the fly, never to a file (a nest image is 1 GB). OpenWrt puts its metadata after the
	# gzip data: gzip warns "trailing garbage" and exits 2, which is fine
	unpack() { gzip -dc "$IMG" 2>/dev/null || [ $? -eq 2 ]; }
	BYTES=$(unpack | wc -c) || die "Cannot unpack $IMG"
	[ "$BYTES" -gt 0 ] || die "Empty image: $IMG"

	for P in $(lsblk -lnpo NAME "$DEV"); do
		if findmnt -rn -S "$P" >/dev/null; then sudo umount "$P"; fi
	done
	echo "Erasing the old partition table, then writing $((BYTES / 1048576)) MiB (sudo asks for your password)..."
	sudo wipefs -a -q "$DEV"
	unpack | sudo dd of="$DEV" bs=4M iflag=fullblock conv=fsync status=progress
	sync
	echo "Reading the card back..."
	sudo blockdev --flushbufs "$DEV"
	unpack | sudo cmp -n "$BYTES" - "$DEV" || die "The card does NOT match the image: write it again, or use another card."
	echo "OK: the card matches the image. You can take it out."
}

# ---------------------------------------------------------------- main

ARG="${1:-}"
MENU=0
[ -z "$ARG" ] && [ -t 0 ] && [ -t 1 ] && MENU=1
CHOSEN=''
case "$ARG" in
	'')    if [ "$MENU" = 1 ]; then menu; else CHOSEN=$(profiles); fi ;;
	all|uses) CHOSEN=$(profiles) ;;
	sd)    write_sd "${2:-}"; exit 0 ;;
	-*|*/*) die "Usage: ./build.sh [all|uses|<role>] | ./build.sh sd [image.img.gz]" ;;
	*)     CHOSEN=$(profiles | lines_of_role "$ARG")
	       [ -n "$CHOSEN" ] || die "No line with role '$ARG' in profiles.txt (roles: $ROLES). Usage: ./build.sh [all|uses|<role>] | ./build.sh sd [image.img.gz]" ;;
esac
[ -n "$CHOSEN" ] || die "Nothing to build."

# Every chosen line checked before anything is downloaded
BAD=''
while read -r ROLE TARGET PROFILE TX2 TX5 UIOK SPEC REST; do
	WHY=$(bad_line "$ROLE" "$TARGET" "$PROFILE" "$TX2" "$TX5" "${UIOK:--}" "${SPEC:-}" "${REST:-}")
	[ -z "$WHY" ] || BAD="$BAD  $ROLE $TARGET $PROFILE: $WHY"$'\n'
done <<<"$CHOSEN"
[ -z "$BAD" ] || die "profiles.txt, line(s) to fix first:"$'\n'"${BAD%$'\n'}"

# What each port and radio will do, shown before anything is built
echo
echo "OpenWrt $VER. To build, and what each interface will do:"
while read -r ROLE TARGET PROFILE TX2 TX5 UIOK SPEC; do
	W=''; [ "$UI" = 1 ] && [ "${UIOK:--}" = ui ] && W='  + web interface'
	echo "  $ROLE on $PROFILE ($TARGET)${SPEC:+, manual}$W"
	uses_long "$(uses_of "$ROLE" "$PROFILE" "$TX2" "$TX5" "${SPEC:-}")"
done <<<"$CHOSEN"
# Every nest image gets the same village address scheme: say so once
if printf '%s\n' "$CHOSEN" | lines_of_role nest | grep -q . ; then
	echo "  Nests: Android needs a non-ULA prefix; the image uses 2001:db8:1::/64, the BENCH range (nest/files)."
fi
[ "$ARG" != uses ] || exit 0
echo "  Version: $VERSION"
case "$VERSION" in
	*-dirty) echo "  Files are changed and not committed: these images can't be found in git later. Commit first to give them a version." ;;
	unversioned) echo "  Not a git checkout: these images have no version." ;;
esac
[ "$CHECK_SIG" = 1 ] || echo "  Skipped on purpose: the check of OpenWrt's signature (CHECK_SIG=0)"
[ "$CHECK_RULES" = 1 ] || echo "  Skipped on purpose: the check of the packages against the hard rules (CHECK_RULES=0)"
if [ "$MENU" = 1 ]; then
	case "$(ask "Go? [Y/n] ")" in [nN]*) exit 0 ;; esac
fi

compgen -G "$HERE/keys/*.pub" >/dev/null || die "Put admin SSH public keys in keys/*.pub first"
MISSING=''
for C in wget zstd tar make gcc perl rsync python3 sha256sum $( [ "$CHECK_SIG" = 0 ] || echo gpgv ); do
	command -v "$C" >/dev/null || MISSING="$MISSING $C"
done
[ -z "$MISSING" ] || die "Missing on this machine:$MISSING (the Image Builder needs them; see its page on openwrt.org)"
mkdir -p "$BUILD" "$OUT"
case "$BUILD" in *[[:space:]]*) die "The build folder $BUILD has a space in its path, which the Image Builder can't handle. Set SPARROWS_BUILD=<a folder without spaces>" ;; esac
[ "$BUILD" = "$HERE/build" ] || echo "This folder's path has a space: the Image Builder works in $BUILD (images still go to $OUT)."
: > "$BUILD/.built"; : > "$BUILD/.skipped"

while read -r ROLE TARGET PROFILE TX2 TX5 UIOK SPEC; do
	build_one "$ROLE" "$TARGET" "$PROFILE" "$TX2" "$TX5" "${UIOK:--}" "${SPEC:-}" </dev/null
done <<<"$CHOSEN"

echo
if [ -s "$BUILD/.skipped" ]; then
	echo "Skipped:"
	sed 's/^/  /' "$BUILD/.skipped"
	echo
fi
[ -s "$BUILD/.built" ] || die "No image was built."
# This version's checksums, for the kit and for anyone who checks an image: SHA256SUMS in its folder
( cd "$OUT/$VERSION" && shopt -s nullglob && IMGS=(*.bin *.img.gz) && sha256sum -- "${IMGS[@]}" > SHA256SUMS )
ln -sfn "$VERSION" "$OUT/latest"
echo "Built now, version $VERSION, in $OUT/$VERSION (also $OUT/latest):"
( cd "$OUT/$VERSION" && while read -r I; do sha256sum -- "$I"; done < "$BUILD/.built" )

if grep -q -e '-ui-' "$BUILD/.built"; then
	echo
	echo "Web interface (images with -ui): it only listens on the unit itself. From your laptop:"
	echo "  ssh -N -L 8080:127.0.0.1:80 root@<station address>    then open http://localhost:8080"
	echo "  The first time, set the root password over SSH:  passwd   (SSH itself stays keys only)"
fi

if [ "$MENU" = 1 ] && grep -q '\.img\.gz$' "$BUILD/.built"; then
	echo
	case "$(ask "Write a Raspberry Pi image to an SD card now? [y/N] ")" in [yY]*) write_sd ;; esac
fi

VersionsVersionsVersiones not yet tested

Added on 2026-11-21. The build tree lives in git, and each build carries the version git gives it: the last tag, the number of commits since, and the commit, like v2026.11.21-3-g1f59fc3. With changes that are not committed, the version ends in -dirty and the build says so: such images can't be found in git later, so commit first. The images go to build/images/<version>/, with a SHA256SUMS file, and build/images/latest points to the newest. Older versions stay, to go back to. On a unit, /etc/sparrows/version and the login text give the version it runs, so git log <that version>.. on the admin laptop shows exactly what changed since. A version that passed the bench gets a tag whose message says what was tested. Each change is one commit, and its message says what changed, why, and whether it was tested. Tried on 2026-11-21 against a stand-in Image Builder; not yet on a unit.

Ajouté le 2026-11-21. L'arbre de build vit dans git, et chaque build porte la version que git lui donne : la dernière étiquette, le nombre de commits depuis, et le commit, comme v2026.11.21-3-g1f59fc3. Avec des changements non commités, la version finit par -dirty et le build le dit : de telles images sont introuvables dans git ensuite, donc commiter d'abord. Les images vont dans build/images/<version>/, avec un fichier SHA256SUMS, et build/images/latest pointe vers la plus récente. Les versions plus anciennes restent, pour revenir en arrière. Sur un boîtier, /etc/sparrows/version et le texte de connexion donnent la version qu'il fait tourner, donc git log <cette version>.. sur le portable d'admin montre exactement ce qui a changé depuis. Une version qui a passé le banc reçoit une étiquette dont le message dit ce qui a été testé. Chaque changement est un commit, et son message dit ce qui change, pourquoi, et si c'est testé. Essayé le 2026-11-21 avec un faux Image Builder ; pas encore sur un boîtier.

Añadido el 2026-11-21. El árbol de construcción vive en git, y cada construcción lleva la versión que git le da: la última etiqueta, el número de commits desde entonces, y el commit, como v2026.11.21-3-g1f59fc3. Con cambios sin commit, la versión acaba en -dirty y la construcción lo dice: esas imágenes no se pueden encontrar luego en git, así que hacer commit primero. Las imágenes van a build/images/<versión>/, con un archivo SHA256SUMS, y build/images/latest apunta a la más reciente. Las versiones anteriores se quedan, para volver atrás. En una unidad, /etc/sparrows/version y el texto de inicio de sesión dan la versión que funciona, así que git log <esa versión>.. en el portátil de administración muestra exactamente lo que cambió desde entonces. Una versión que pasó el banco recibe una etiqueta cuyo mensaje dice lo que se probó. Cada cambio es un commit, y su mensaje dice qué cambia, por qué, y si está probado. Probado el 2026-11-21 con un Image Builder falso; aún no en una unidad.

admin laptop, then a unit
# On the admin laptop, in the git checkout: the version the next build will carry
git describe --tags --always --dirty        # e.g. v2026.11.21-3-g1f59fc3 ; "-dirty" = changes not committed
git status --short                          # what is not committed yet
git commit -am "What changed, why, and tested or not"   # one change per commit, then build

# After a build: one folder per version, latest -> the newest, a SHA256SUMS in each
ls -l build/images/
cat build/images/latest/SHA256SUMS

# On a unit: the version it runs (also in the login text)
cat /etc/sparrows/version                   # SPARROWS_VERSION, OPENWRT, BUILT, LINE (its line of profiles.txt)

# Back on the laptop: what changed since the version a unit runs
git log --oneline <its version>..           # the commits, one line each
git diff --stat <its version>.. -- sparrows-openwrt/     # the files

# A version that passed the bench gets a tag: its message says what was tested
git tag -a v2026.11.21 -m "Pi 4 nest+feeder: RAs, DNS and Wi-Fi tested on the bench"
git push origin v2026.11.21

# Undo: flash the images of an earlier version, from its folder
ls build/images/<earlier version>/

Web interface, optionalInterface web, en optionInterfaz web, opcional not yet tested

Added on 2026-11-18. Off by default. With w in the menu (or UI=1), images get LuCI, OpenWrt's web interface: radios, signal, clients and logs in a browser, with no commands to learn. Only on devices marked ui in profiles.txt: small ones (8 MB of flash, 64 MB of RAM, like the CPE510) are built without it and the build says so (hard rule). If an image comes out too big for the flash, that one is skipped and the others are built. Images with it have -ui in their name. It comes without LuCI's firewall page: the usual LuCI bundle (luci-light) would bring the firewall back, which the hard rules forbid (found 2026-11-20), so the image takes LuCI's parts without it.

Ajoutée le 2026-11-18. Coupée par défaut. Avec w dans le menu (ou UI=1), les images reçoivent LuCI, l'interface web d'OpenWrt : radios, signal, clients et journaux dans un navigateur, sans commande à apprendre. Seulement sur les appareils marqués ui dans profiles.txt : les petits (8 Mo de flash, 64 Mo de RAM, comme le CPE510) sont construits sans elle et le build le dit (règle dure). Si une image sort trop grosse pour la flash, celle-là est sautée et les autres sont construites. Les images qui l'ont portent -ui dans leur nom. Elle vient sans la page pare-feu de LuCI : le paquet LuCI habituel (luci-light) ramènerait le pare-feu, que les règles dures interdisent (trouvé le 2026-11-20), donc l'image prend les morceaux de LuCI sans lui.

Añadida el 2026-11-18. Apagada por defecto. Con w en el menú (o UI=1), las imágenes llevan LuCI, la interfaz web de OpenWrt: radios, señal, clientes y registros en un navegador, sin comandos que aprender. Solo en los aparatos marcados ui en profiles.txt: los pequeños (8 MB de flash, 64 MB de RAM, como la CPE510) se construyen sin ella y la construcción lo dice (regla firme). Si una imagen sale demasiado grande para la flash, esa se salta y las demás se construyen. Las imágenes que la llevan tienen -ui en su nombre. Viene sin la página de cortafuegos de LuCI: el paquete habitual de LuCI (luci-light) volvería a traer el cortafuegos, que las reglas firmes prohíben (descubierto el 2026-11-20), así que la imagen toma las piezas de LuCI sin él.

It listens on the unit itself only, never on the open Wi-Fi: you reach it through SSH with your admin key (first lines of the file below). LuCI asks for the root password, which every image locks, so set one once over SSH with passwd. SSH itself stays keys only, so the password is useless without the key. A change made in LuCI is a test, as on the command line: to keep it, put it in the build tree and rebuild.

Elle n'écoute que sur le boîtier lui-même, jamais sur le Wi-Fi ouvert : on l'atteint par SSH avec sa clé d'admin (premières lignes du fichier ci-dessous). LuCI demande le mot de passe root, que chaque image verrouille : on en met un une fois par SSH avec passwd. SSH reste en clés seulement, donc le mot de passe ne sert à rien sans la clé. Un changement fait dans LuCI est un essai, comme en ligne de commande : pour le garder, on le met dans l'arbre de build et on reconstruit.

Solo escucha en la propia unidad, nunca en el Wi-Fi abierto: se llega a ella por SSH con la clave de administración (primeras líneas del archivo de abajo). LuCI pide la contraseña de root, que cada imagen bloquea, así que se pone una vez por SSH con passwd. SSH sigue siendo solo con claves, así que la contraseña no sirve de nada sin la clave. Un cambio hecho en LuCI es una prueba, como en la línea de comandos: para conservarlo, se pone en el árbol de construcción y se reconstruye.

How to reach it. The same reminder is printed at the end of every build that made a -ui image, and at every SSH login on the unit.

Comment y accéder. Le même rappel s'affiche à la fin de chaque build qui a fait une image -ui, et à chaque connexion SSH sur le boîtier.

Cómo llegar a ella. El mismo recordatorio aparece al final de cada construcción que haya hecho una imagen -ui, y en cada conexión SSH a la unidad.

laptop, then the station
# On your laptop: a tunnel through SSH, with your admin key. Leave it open.
ssh -N -L 8080:127.0.0.1:80 root@<station address>
# Then open http://localhost:8080 in the browser.
# First time only, on the station over SSH: the root password LuCI asks for
passwd

# Verify on the station: the web server listens on the unit itself only
netstat -ltn | grep ':80 '                 # 127.0.0.1:80 and ::1:80 only, never 0.0.0.0:80 or :::80
# From another laptop on the village: must FAIL
curl -m 5 'http://[<station address>]/'

# Undo: flash the same role without -ui (sysupgrade -n), or on the station:
/etc/init.d/uhttpd disable && /etc/init.d/uhttpd stop
ui/files/etc/uci-defaults/95-sparrows-ui
#!/bin/sh
# Sparrows-Network: web interface (LuCI). Only in images built with the web interface on.
# It listens on the unit itself only, never on the village: reach it through SSH with the admin key.
#   On the laptop:  ssh -N -L 8080:127.0.0.1:80 root@<station address>
#   Then open:      http://localhost:8080
# LuCI asks for the root password, which every image locks. Set one once, over SSH:  passwd
# SSH itself stays keys only. `sysupgrade -n` and `firstboot` lock the password again.
# A change made in LuCI is a test: to keep it, put it in the build tree and rebuild.
# Status: NOT YET TESTED.

uci -q delete uhttpd.main.listen_http
uci -q delete uhttpd.main.listen_https
uci add_list uhttpd.main.listen_http='127.0.0.1:80'
uci add_list uhttpd.main.listen_http='[::1]:80'
uci set uhttpd.main.redirect_https='0'
uci commit uhttpd

# Reminder at every SSH login (once in the banner, even if this file runs again)
grep -q 'Sparrows web interface' /etc/banner 2>/dev/null || cat >> /etc/banner <<'EOF'
 Sparrows web interface (LuCI): only through SSH. On your laptop:
   ssh -N -L 8080:127.0.0.1:80 root@<this station>   then open http://localhost:8080
   First time: set the root password here with  passwd   (SSH stays keys only)

EOF

exit 0

Write a Raspberry Pi image to an SD cardÉcrire une image Raspberry Pi sur une carte SDEscribir una imagen de Raspberry Pi en una tarjeta SD not yet tested

Added on 2026-11-18. A Pi takes its system from an SD card, not from flash. ./build.sh sd (or s in the menu, or yes after a build that made a Pi image) writes the image to a card: it asks which reader holds the card, shows what is on it, and writes nothing until you type the disk's name. Writing the image is also the formatting: the partitions come with the image, and everything that was on the card is gone. Then it reads the card back and compares it with the image. Tried on 2026-11-18 on a stand-in disk (a loop device), not yet on a real card. OpenWrt makes two kinds of Pi image, squashfs and ext4; the build keeps only squashfs, because going back to the image's defaults (firstboot) only works with it.

Ajouté le 2026-11-18. Un Pi prend son système sur une carte SD, pas sur une flash. ./build.sh sd (ou s dans le menu, ou oui après un build qui a fait une image de Pi) écrit l'image sur une carte : il demande quel lecteur tient la carte, montre ce qu'il y a dessus, et n'écrit rien tant qu'on n'a pas tapé le nom du disque. Écrire l'image, c'est aussi le formatage : les partitions viennent avec l'image, et tout ce qui était sur la carte disparaît. Ensuite il relit la carte et la compare à l'image. Essayé le 2026-11-18 sur un faux disque (un périphérique loop), pas encore sur une vraie carte. OpenWrt fait deux sortes d'images de Pi, squashfs et ext4 ; le build ne garde que squashfs, car le retour aux réglages de l'image (firstboot) ne marche qu'avec lui.

Añadido el 2026-11-18. Una Pi toma su sistema de una tarjeta SD, no de una flash. ./build.sh sd (o s en el menú, o sí después de una construcción que haya hecho una imagen de Pi) escribe la imagen en una tarjeta: pregunta qué lector tiene la tarjeta, muestra lo que hay en ella y no escribe nada hasta que se teclea el nombre del disco. Escribir la imagen es también el formateo: las particiones vienen con la imagen, y todo lo que había en la tarjeta desaparece. Después relee la tarjeta y la compara con la imagen. Probado el 2026-11-18 en un disco de prueba (un dispositivo loop), aún no en una tarjeta de verdad. OpenWrt hace dos tipos de imagen de Pi, squashfs y ext4; la construcción solo guarda squashfs, porque volver a los ajustes de la imagen (firstboot) solo funciona con él.

Erase risk: only removable, USB and MMC disks are offered, never one that holds the laptop's system. Still, read the size and the model before typing the name. A 1 TB "card" is your backup disk.

Risque d'effacement : seuls les disques amovibles, USB et MMC sont proposés, jamais celui qui porte le système du portable. Lire quand même la taille et le modèle avant de taper le nom. Une « carte » de 1 To, c'est votre disque de sauvegarde.

Riesgo de borrado: solo se ofrecen discos extraíbles, USB y MMC, nunca el que lleva el sistema del portátil. Aun así, leer el tamaño y el modelo antes de teclear el nombre. Una «tarjeta» de 1 TB es tu disco de copias.

admin laptop, SD card in a reader
# On the admin laptop, after building a Raspberry Pi image (or s in the menu)
./build.sh sd                          # the factory .img.gz in build/images/latest (asks which, if several)
./build.sh sd path/to/image.img.gz     # any other image
# It lists only removable, USB and MMC disks, never one that holds / /boot /home /usr /var or swap.
# It shows what is on the card, asks you to TYPE its name, writes, then reads the card back.

# The same by hand, if the script can't run. Replace sdX, and check it twice with lsblk.
lsblk -po NAME,SIZE,TRAN,MODEL,MOUNTPOINT
gzip -dc image.img.gz > card.img       # "trailing garbage ignored" is normal for OpenWrt images
sudo umount /dev/sdX?* 2>/dev/null
sudo dd if=card.img of=/dev/sdX bs=4M conv=fsync status=progress
sudo blockdev --flushbufs /dev/sdX
sudo cmp -n "$(stat -c %s card.img)" card.img /dev/sdX && echo "OK: the card matches"

2. Base shared by every station2. Base commune à toutes les stations2. Base común a todas las estaciones

common/files/etc/uci-defaults/10-sparrows-common
#!/bin/sh
# Sparrows-Network: base shared by every OpenWrt station, whatever its role.
# No IPv4, no DHCP, no RA (except on a nest: 91-sparrows-nest), no firewall, SSH keys only. The ports and radios get their uses in
# 90-sparrows-station, from /etc/sparrows/station.
# Runs once at first boot (and again after `firstboot && reboot`).
# Status: NOT YET TESTED.

. /etc/sparrows/station          # ROLE, TX2, TX5, PORTS, USE2, USE5, RETICULUM, NEST (written by build.sh)

# --- Name: <role>-<last 4 hex of the MAC>, e.g. perch-3fa2. Same image on every unit ---
# MACDEV (in /etc/sparrows/station) = where the fixed MAC is: eth0 on most units; phy0 (the radio)
# on a unit with no Ethernet of its own, like the Pi Zero 2 W
case "${MACDEV:-eth0}" in
	phy*) MAC=$(cat "/sys/class/ieee80211/$MACDEV/macaddress" 2>/dev/null) ;;
	*)    MAC=$(cat "/sys/class/net/${MACDEV:-eth0}/address" 2>/dev/null) ;;
esac
[ -n "$MAC" ] || MAC=$(cat /sys/class/net/*/address 2>/dev/null | grep -v '^00:00:00' | head -n1)
ID=$(echo "$MAC" | tr -d ':' | tail -c 5)
uci set system.@system[0].hostname="$ROLE-$ID"
uci set system.@system[0].log_size='16'
uci set system.@system[0].cronloglevel='9'
uci commit system

# --- Login text: /etc/sparrows/banner (edit it in the build tree), filled in for this unit ---
# What it does, from its uses, e.g. "hedge: ports wire, 2.4 GHz flyway+feeder, 5 GHz feeder".
# No | in these texts: sed below uses it as its separator
use() { [ -z "$1" ] || [ "$1" = off ] && echo off || echo "$1" | tr ' ' '+'; }
WHAT="$ROLE: ports $PORTS, 2.4 GHz $(use "$USE2"), 5 GHz $(use "$USE5")"
[ "$RETICULUM" != 1 ] || WHAT="$WHAT, Reticulum"
[ "${NEST:-0}" != 1 ] || WHAT="$WHAT, nest (RAs, DNS, welcome page, message store)"
HINT=''
case " $USE5 " in *" flyway "*) HINT="$HINT  perch status  perch ap" ;; esac
case " $USE2 $USE5 " in *" flyway "*) HINT="$HINT  batctl n  batctl o" ;; esac
case " $USE2 $USE5 " in *" feeder "*|*" join "*) HINT="$HINT  iw dev" ;; esac
[ "$RETICULUM" != 1 ] || HINT="$HINT  rns status"
[ "${NEST:-0}" != 1 ] || HINT="$HINT  logread -e odhcpd  /etc/init.d/lxmd status"
REL=$( [ -f /etc/openwrt_release ] && . /etc/openwrt_release; echo "${DISTRIB_DESCRIPTION:-OpenWrt}")
# The build tree's version this image was made from (build.sh: git describe). On the admin laptop,
# "git log <version>.." shows what changed since
SPARROWS_VERSION=$( [ -f /etc/sparrows/version ] && . /etc/sparrows/version; echo "${SPARROWS_VERSION:-unversioned}")
REL="$REL, Sparrows $SPARROWS_VERSION"
[ -z "$HINT" ] || HINT="Useful here:${HINT# }"
sed -e "s|@NAME@|$ROLE-$ID|" -e "s|@ROLE@|$WHAT|" -e "s|@RELEASE@|$REL|" -e "s|@HINT@|$HINT|" \
	/etc/sparrows/banner > /etc/banner

# --- Admin: SSH keys only (P1). Failsafe mode still works for recovery ---
passwd -l root >/dev/null 2>&1
uci set dropbear.@dropbear[0].PasswordAuth='off'
uci set dropbear.@dropbear[0].RootPasswordAuth='off'
uci commit dropbear

# --- Network: br-lan = plain bridge. Management address by SLAAC from the nests' RAs ---
# odhcp6c is an RA *client*: it never sends RAs. With no nest up, link-local still works.
uci -q delete network.globals.ula_prefix
uci -q delete network.wan
uci -q delete network.wan6
uci -q delete network.lan.ipaddr
uci -q delete network.lan.netmask
uci -q delete network.lan.ip6assign
uci -q delete network.lan.ip6addr
uci -q delete network.lan.dns
uci set network.lan.proto='dhcpv6'
uci set network.lan.reqaddress='none'
uci set network.lan.reqprefix='no'
uci commit network

exit 0

Login textTexte de connexionTexto de inicio de sesión not yet tested

Added on 2026-11-19. Instead of OpenWrt's logo, every unit greets you at SSH login with the Sparrows text: its name, what it does, the OpenWrt version, where the handbook is, the reminder that a change by hand is only a test, and a few commands that are useful on that role. The text lives in one file of the build tree, common/files/etc/sparrows/banner. At first boot, the base above fills in the unit's own name and role and writes it to /etc/banner, which OpenWrt shows at each login. To change it, edit that one file and rebuild.

Ajouté le 2026-11-19. Le texte est dans un seul fichier de l'arbre de build, common/files/etc/sparrows/banner. Au premier démarrage, la base ci-dessus y met le nom et le rôle du boîtier et l'écrit dans /etc/banner, qu'OpenWrt affiche à chaque connexion. Pour le changer, on modifie ce fichier et on reconstruit.

Añadido el 2026-11-19.    / )\     @RELEASE@
  --"-"--
 Handbook: http://sparrows.lan/  (the Sparrows Tao)
 This unit runs what the build tree makes. A change made here by hand is a test:
 to keep it, put it in sparrows-openwrt/ and rebuild.
 @HINT@

3. Perch image3. Image de perchoir3. Imagen de percha

The same radio settings as the per-node script that ran on the bench on 2026-11-14, with nothing left to set per unit (name and address come from the MAC), plus bridge loop avoidance. Every perch boots as a client: a client only transmits after hearing its AP, so a fresh perch is harmless. On the one perch per flyway that must carry the radar duty, perch ap makes it the AP. The tool refuses channels 120 to 128 and the indoor channels. The image carries batctl-default, not batctl-tiny: batctl n, o, ping, traceroute and tp all work, and the CPE510 image built that way fits in its 8 MB of flash (2026-11-14). The flyway key is checked twice: by build.sh, and again at first boot, which logs an error if it was edited by hand.

Les mêmes réglages radio que le script par nœud qui a tourné sur le banc le 2026-11-14, sans plus rien à régler par boîtier (le nom et l'adresse viennent de la MAC), plus la détection de boucles. Chaque perchoir démarre en client : un client n'émet qu'après avoir entendu son AP, donc un perchoir neuf ne gêne personne. Sur le seul perchoir de chaque voie qui doit porter le devoir radar, perch ap en fait l'AP. L'outil refuse les canaux 120 à 128 et les canaux d'intérieur. L'image embarque batctl-default, pas batctl-tiny : batctl n, o, ping, traceroute et tp marchent tous, et l'image du CPE510 construite ainsi tient dans ses 8 Mo de flash (2026-11-14). La clé de la voie est vérifiée deux fois : par build.sh, puis au premier démarrage, qui journalise une erreur si elle a été modifiée à la main.

Los mismos ajustes de radio que el script por nodo que funcionó en el banco el 2026-11-14, sin nada que ajustar por unidad (el nombre y la dirección salen de la MAC), más la detección de bucles. Cada percha arranca como cliente: un cliente solo transmite después de oír a su AP, así que una percha nueva es inofensiva. En la única percha de cada ruta que debe cargar con el deber de radar, perch ap la convierte en el AP. La herramienta rechaza los canales 120 a 128 y los canales de interior. La imagen lleva batctl-default, no batctl-tiny: batctl n, o, ping, traceroute y tp funcionan todos, y la imagen del CPE510 construida así cabe en sus 8 MB de flash (2026-11-14). La clave de la ruta se comprueba dos veces: por build.sh, y otra vez en el primer arranque, que registra un error si se editó a mano.

First boot: the shared file in What each port and radio does, with this line of uses.txt: perch wire off flyway -.

Premier démarrage : le fichier commun de Ce que fait chaque port et chaque radio, avec cette ligne de uses.txt : perch wire off flyway -.

Primer arranque: el archivo común de Lo que hace cada puerto y cada radio, con esta línea de uses.txt: perch wire off flyway -.

perch/files/usr/sbin/perch
#!/bin/sh
# perch: switch this perch between flyway roles, or show its state.
#   perch ap  [channel]   this end beacons and does the radar duty (one AP per flyway)
#   perch sta [channel]   this end follows its AP (default after flashing)
#   perch status
# Channel: outdoor FR, HT20 = 100 104 108 112 116 132 136 140 (120-128 forbidden: weather radar).

OK_CHANNELS='100 104 108 112 116 132 136 140'

R=$(uci -q get wireless.flyway.device) || { echo "no flyway interface: not a perch image?" >&2; exit 1; }

case "$1" in
ap|sta)
	if [ -n "$2" ]; then
		case " $OK_CHANNELS " in
			*" $2 "*) uci set wireless.$R.channel="$2" ;;
			*) echo "channel $2 not allowed outdoors. Use one of: $OK_CHANNELS" >&2; exit 1 ;;
		esac
	fi
	uci set wireless.flyway.mode="$1"
	if [ "$1" = ap ]; then uci set wireless.flyway.hidden='1'; else uci -q delete wireless.flyway.hidden; fi
	uci commit wireless
	wifi
	echo "flyway: $1 on channel $(uci get wireless.$R.channel)."
	if [ "$1" = ap ]; then
		echo "The AP listens 60 s for radar before it transmits: wait for DFS-CAC-COMPLETED in logread."
	fi
	;;
status)
	echo "role: $(uci get wireless.flyway.mode)  channel: $(uci get wireless.$R.channel)  txpower: $(uci -q get wireless.$R.txpower) dBm"
	iw dev 2>/dev/null | grep -e Interface -e channel -e txpower
	batctl if
	batctl n          # neighbours on the flyway (needs batctl-default, not -tiny)
	logread | grep -i -e cac -e connected | tail -n 5
	;;
*)
	sed -n '2,7s/^# \{0,1\}//p' "$0"
	exit 1
	;;
esac
install, verify, undo
# Find the new perch from a laptop on its cable
ping -6 -c2 ff02::1%eth0; ip -6 neigh show dev eth0
ssh root@fe80::<from MAC>%eth0

# Every perch boots as a client. On the one perch per flyway that does the radar duty:
perch ap                # or: perch ap 116   (refuses 120-128 and indoor channels)

# After ~70 s, on both ends
perch status            # AP: DFS-CAC-COMPLETED, then AP-STA-CONNECTED ... auth_alg=sae
batctl if               # phy0-ap0 / phy0-sta0: active
batctl n                # the perch at the other end of the flyway
batctl o                # every batman node of the village
batctl bl               # bridge loop avoidance: enabled
batctl tp <other MAC>   # throughput over the flyway (batman layer)
ip -6 addr show br-lan  # fe80:: (+ fd7e:4c1a:9b20:1:... once a nest sends RAs), no inet

# Live signal while aiming the antennas (good: -40 to -70 dBm). Low rates on an idle link are normal
watch -n1 "iw dev phy0-sta0 link | grep -e signal -e bitrate"

# Undo
perch sta               # back to client
firstboot && reboot     # back to the image's defaults

The flyway passphrase is read from secrets/flyway.key at build time, so it sits inside every perch image: keep perch images private, never publish them. Layer 1 doesn't rely on that passphrase, but it stays P1 debt until each flyway has its own key.

La phrase de passe de la voie est lue dans secrets/flyway.key au moment du build : elle se trouve donc dans chaque image de perchoir. Garder ces images privées, ne jamais les publier. La couche 1 ne s'appuie pas sur cette phrase de passe, mais elle reste une dette envers P1 tant que chaque voie n'a pas sa propre clé.

La frase de paso de la ruta se lee de secrets/flyway.key al construir, así que está dentro de cada imagen de percha: mantener privadas las imágenes de percha, no publicarlas nunca. La capa 1 no se apoya en esa frase de paso, pero sigue siendo una deuda con P1 hasta que cada ruta tenga su propia clave.

Move the two bench perches to the standardPasser les deux perchoirs du banc au standardPasar las dos perchas del banco al estándar not yet tested

The two CPE510s on the bench still carry the old per-node script: fixed names node1, node2 and fixed addresses ::42, ::43. Flashing the perch image without keeping the config brings them to the standard. A fresh CPE510 takes the factory image from the stock web page, or the sysupgrade image from any OpenWrt.

Les deux CPE510 du banc portent encore l'ancien script par nœud : noms fixes node1, node2 et adresses fixes ::42, ::43. Flasher l'image de perchoir sans garder la configuration les amène au standard. Un CPE510 neuf prend l'image factory depuis la page web d'origine, ou l'image sysupgrade depuis n'importe quel OpenWrt.

Los dos CPE510 del banco llevan todavía el viejo script por nodo: nombres fijos node1, node2 y direcciones fijas ::42, ::43. Flashear la imagen de percha sin conservar la configuración los lleva al estándar. Un CPE510 nuevo toma la imagen factory desde la página web original, o la imagen sysupgrade desde cualquier OpenWrt.

Lock-out risk: after the flash the perch accepts SSH keys only and answers on its MAC-based addresses only. Check that your key is in keys/ before building, and do it with the unit on a cable.

Risque de se retrouver dehors : après le flash, le perchoir n'accepte que des clés SSH et ne répond que sur ses adresses tirées de la MAC. Vérifier que sa clé est dans keys/ avant de construire, et le faire avec le boîtier sur câble.

Riesgo de quedarse fuera: después del flasheo la percha solo acepta claves SSH y solo responde en sus direcciones sacadas de la MAC. Comprobar que su clave está en keys/ antes de construir, y hacerlo con la unidad conectada por cable.

laptop, perch, nest
# Not yet tested. Do it on the bench, the client end (old node2) first, the AP end (old node1) last.
# LOCK-OUT RISK: after this the perch takes SSH keys only, and its old fixed address is gone.
scp -O build/images/latest/*sparrows-perch*cpe510-v3*sysupgrade.bin 'root@[fd7e:4c1a:9b20:1::43]:/tmp/fw.bin'
ssh root@fd7e:4c1a:9b20:1::43
sysupgrade -T /tmp/fw.bin          # checks the image, flashes nothing
sysupgrade -n -v /tmp/fw.bin       # -n drops the old config, so the image's first-boot defaults run

# Back in by link-local, with the key (address from the MAC on the sticker)
ssh root@fe80::<from MAC>%eth0
uci get system.@system[0].hostname # perch-xxxx

# Same on the other perch (::42), then on that one only:
perch ap

# On the nest: delete the two host-record lines for node1 and node2 in /etc/dnsmasq.d/mesh.conf, then
sudo dnsmasq --test && sudo systemctl restart dnsmasq

4. Feeder image, example: EAP225-Outdoor4. Image de mangeoire, exemple : EAP225-Outdoor4. Imagen de comedero, ejemplo: EAP225-Outdoor

Open SSID on both bands, clients can reach each other (Reticulum works phone to phone), multicast is sent to each client as unicast so RAs arrive reliably, no per-client log lines.

SSID ouvert sur les deux bandes, les clients se voient entre eux (Reticulum marche de téléphone à téléphone), le multicast est envoyé à chaque client en unicast pour que les RA arrivent de façon fiable, aucune ligne de journal par client.

SSID abierto en las dos bandas, los clientes se alcanzan entre sí (Reticulum funciona de teléfono a teléfono), la multidifusión se envía a cada cliente como unidifusión para que los RA lleguen de forma fiable, ninguna línea de registro por cliente.

Three units arrived on 2026-11-14: two v1 and one v3. Both versions are supported by OpenWrt and are nearly the same box: the same SoC with its 2.4 GHz radio, a QCA9886 for 5 GHz, 128 MB of RAM and 16 MB of flash; the v3 differs by its Ethernet chip. Each gets the image built for its own version: check the label before flashing.

Trois boîtiers sont arrivés le 2026-11-14 : deux v1 et un v3. Les deux versions sont prises en charge par OpenWrt et c'est presque le même boîtier : le même SoC avec sa radio 2,4 GHz, un QCA9886 pour le 5 GHz, 128 Mo de RAM et 16 Mo de flash ; le v3 ne diffère que par sa puce Ethernet. Chacun reçoit l'image construite pour sa propre version : vérifier l'étiquette avant de flasher.

Llegaron tres unidades el 2026-11-14: dos v1 y una v3. OpenWrt soporta las dos versiones y son casi la misma caja: el mismo SoC con su radio de 2,4 GHz, un QCA9886 para 5 GHz, 128 MB de RAM y 16 MB de flash; la v3 se distingue por su chip Ethernet. Cada una recibe la imagen construida para su versión: mirar la etiqueta antes de flashear.

Premier démarrage : le fichier commun de Ce que fait chaque port et chaque radio, avec cette ligne de uses.txt : feeder wire feeder feeder -.

Primer arranque: el archivo común de Lo que hace cada puerto y cada radio, con esta línea de uses.txt: feeder wire feeder feeder -.

Flash the factory image as described in Flash an EAP225-Outdoor below, then plug the feeder into the site switch. Wait about 70 s after boot for the radar check on 5 GHz.

Flasher l'image factory comme décrit dans Flasher un EAP225-Outdoor plus bas, puis brancher la mangeoire sur le switch du site. Attendre environ 70 s après le démarrage, le temps du test radar en 5 GHz.

Flashear la imagen factory como se describe en Flashear un EAP225-Outdoor más abajo, y enchufar el comedero al switch del sitio. Esperar unos 70 s después del arranque, por la comprobación de radar en 5 GHz.

verify
# From a nest or a laptop on the mesh: find the feeder by its MAC
ping -6 -c2 ff02::1%eth0; ip -6 neigh show dev eth0
ssh root@fe80::<from MAC>%eth0

# On the feeder (hostname feeder-<last 4 hex of the MAC>)
ip -6 addr show br-lan          # fe80:: (+ fd7e:4c1a:9b20:1:... once a nest sends RAs), no inet
iw dev                          # two APs, ch 132 / auto, txpower 23 / 17
logread | grep -i cac           # DFS-CAC-COMPLETED on 5 GHz
ps | grep -e dnsmasq -e odhcpd  # nothing

# From a laptop on the SSID: only the nests should answer
rdisc6 wlan0
undo
firstboot && reboot             # back to this image's defaults
# Locked out: failsafe mode (reset button during boot) -> 192.168.1.1
# Back to stock firmware: procedure on the OpenWrt wiki page of the device

Two open points. Any phone on the open SSID can send a rogue RA, and the bridge would spread it. Dropping RAs that arrive from the Wi-Fi side would fix it, but that is an exception to "never filter ICMPv6": not decided, not in the image. And TP-Link's CE figure for this unit is 27 dBm EIRP on 5 GHz, because it has no transmit power control (TPC), hence 23 dBm at the radio in profiles.txt. If the CPE510 has no TPC either, the same rule gives 14 dBm at its radio, not 17. To check against the French rules before perches go on masts: it is one number in profiles.txt.

Deux points ouverts. N'importe quel téléphone sur le SSID ouvert peut envoyer un faux RA, et le pont le propagerait. Jeter les RA qui arrivent du côté Wi-Fi réglerait le problème, mais c'est une exception à « ne jamais filtrer ICMPv6 » : pas décidé, pas dans l'image. Et la valeur CE de TP-Link pour ce boîtier est de 27 dBm de PIRE en 5 GHz, parce qu'il n'a pas de contrôle de puissance d'émission (TPC), d'où 23 dBm à la radio dans profiles.txt. Si le CPE510 n'a pas de TPC non plus, la même règle donne 14 dBm à sa radio, pas 17. À vérifier dans les règles françaises avant de monter les perchoirs sur les mâts : c'est un seul nombre dans profiles.txt.

Dos puntos abiertos. Cualquier teléfono en el SSID abierto puede enviar un RA falso, y el puente lo propagaría. Descartar los RA que llegan del lado Wi-Fi lo arreglaría, pero es una excepción a «no filtrar nunca ICMPv6»: sin decidir, no está en la imagen. Y la cifra CE de TP-Link para esta unidad es de 27 dBm de PIRE en 5 GHz, porque no tiene control de potencia de transmisión (TPC), de ahí los 23 dBm en la radio en profiles.txt. Si el CPE510 tampoco tiene TPC, la misma regla da 14 dBm en su radio, no 17. A comprobar con las reglas francesas antes de subir las perchas a los mástiles: es un solo número en profiles.txt.

Feeder on a Raspberry Pi Zero 2 WMangeoire sur un Raspberry Pi Zero 2 WComedero en una Raspberry Pi Zero 2 W not yet tested

So faron 2026-11-21 the feeder image (with the web interface) was built with the real Image Builder: Wi-Fi firmware fetched and checked, package rules passed, a 78 MB factory image. ./build.sh sd wrote it to a card and read it back identical. Not yet booted on the Pi.

Added on 2026-11-19. The Zero 2 W is not a standard OpenWrt device, so it has a folder of its own, devices/rpi-zero-2-w/, and profiles.txt names that folder instead of an OpenWrt profile. What differs:

Jusqu'icile 2026-11-21, l'image de mangeoire (avec l'interface web) a été construite avec le vrai Image Builder : firmware Wi-Fi récupéré et vérifié, règles des paquets passées, une image factory de 78 Mo. ./build.sh sd l'a écrite sur une carte et relue identique. Pas encore démarrée sur le Pi.

Ajouté le 2026-11-19. Le Zero 2 W n'est pas un appareil OpenWrt standard : il a donc son propre dossier, devices/rpi-zero-2-w/, et profiles.txt nomme ce dossier au lieu d'un profil OpenWrt. Ce qui change :

Hasta ahorael 2026-11-21 la imagen de comedero (con la interfaz web) se construyó con el Image Builder de verdad: firmware Wi-Fi descargado y comprobado, reglas de paquetes superadas, una imagen factory de 78 MB. ./build.sh sd la escribió en una tarjeta y la releyó idéntica. Aún no arrancada en la Pi.

Añadido el 2026-11-19. La Zero 2 W no es un aparato OpenWrt estándar, así que tiene su propia carpeta, devices/rpi-zero-2-w/, y profiles.txt nombra esa carpeta en lugar de un perfil OpenWrt. Lo que cambia:

  • Image: OpenWrt has none of its own. The Pi 3 image (rpi-3) boots it: same chip.
  • Wi-Fi firmware: OpenWrt doesn't ship it, because its licence is unclear. The build fetches it from Raspberry Pi's firmware repository at a fixed version and checks every file. Images that contain it are for the village's own units: don't put them in a public kit.
  • No Ethernet on the board: the village cable goes into a USB Ethernet adapter, on the port marked USB through an OTG cable or hub. Drivers for the common adapters are in the image.
  • MAC: the adapter can be swapped, so the name and the address come from the radio's MAC.
  • Radio: 2.4 GHz only, a fixed channel (6), because its driver can't pick one by itself. So it can be a feeder (with the adapter) or a tree (Wi-Fi client, no adapter), not a perch or a hedge.
  • Image : OpenWrt n'en a pas pour lui. L'image du Pi 3 (rpi-3) le démarre : même puce.
  • Firmware Wi-Fi : OpenWrt ne le fournit pas, car sa licence n'est pas claire. Le build le récupère dans le dépôt de firmware de Raspberry Pi, à une version fixe, et vérifie chaque fichier. Les images qui le contiennent sont pour les boîtiers du village : ne pas les mettre dans un kit public.
  • Pas d'Ethernet sur la carte : le câble du village va dans un adaptateur Ethernet USB, sur le port marqué USB, par un câble OTG ou un hub. Les pilotes des adaptateurs courants sont dans l'image.
  • MAC : l'adaptateur peut être changé, donc le nom et l'adresse viennent de la MAC de la radio.
  • Radio : 2,4 GHz seulement, sur un canal fixe (6), car son pilote ne sait pas en choisir un seul. Il peut donc être une mangeoire (avec l'adaptateur) ou un arbre (client Wi-Fi, sans adaptateur), pas un perchoir ni une haie.
  • Imagen: OpenWrt no tiene una propia. La imagen de la Pi 3 (rpi-3) la arranca: mismo chip.
  • Firmware Wi-Fi: OpenWrt no lo incluye, porque su licencia no está clara. La construcción lo descarga del repositorio de firmware de Raspberry Pi, en una versión fija, y comprueba cada archivo. Las imágenes que lo llevan son para las unidades del pueblo: no ponerlas en un kit público.
  • Sin Ethernet en la placa: el cable del pueblo va a un adaptador Ethernet USB, en el puerto marcado USB, con un cable OTG o un hub. Los controladores de los adaptadores comunes están en la imagen.
  • MAC: el adaptador se puede cambiar, así que el nombre y la dirección vienen de la MAC de la radio.
  • Radio: solo 2,4 GHz, en un canal fijo (6), porque su controlador no sabe elegir uno solo. Así que puede ser comedero (con el adaptador) o árbol (cliente Wi-Fi, sin adaptador), no percha ni seto.

Small: its Wi-Fi chip serves only a few phones at a time, and may not send multicast to each client as unicast, so RAs can arrive late. Fine for a corner of the village, not for the square. Build it with ./build.sh, pick the rpi-zero-2-w line, then write the card with ./build.sh sd.

Petit : sa puce Wi-Fi ne sert que quelques téléphones à la fois, et n'envoie peut-être pas le multicast à chaque client en unicast, donc les RA peuvent arriver en retard. Bien pour un coin du village, pas pour la place. Le construire avec ./build.sh, choisir la ligne rpi-zero-2-w, puis écrire la carte avec ./build.sh sd.

Pequeño: su chip Wi-Fi solo atiende a unos pocos teléfonos a la vez, y puede que no envíe la multidifusión a cada cliente como unidifusión, así que los RA pueden llegar tarde. Bien para un rincón del pueblo, no para la plaza. Construirlo con ./build.sh, elegir la línea rpi-zero-2-w, y luego escribir la tarjeta con ./build.sh sd.

devices/rpi-zero-2-w/device.conf
# Raspberry Pi Zero 2 W: not a standard OpenWrt device. build.sh reads this file when a line of
# profiles.txt names "rpi-zero-2-w" in its profile column.
# Status: NOT YET TESTED on the unit (written 2026-11-19).

# OpenWrt has no image of its own for it: the Pi 3 image boots it (same SoC, BCM2710).
IB_PROFILE='rpi-3'

# What its radio can do (uses.txt): access point and client, but no 802.11s mesh in its driver,
# so no flyway. The build refuses a flyway on it. One band only: 2.4 GHz.
CAN2='feeder join'
CAN5=''

# No Ethernet on the board: the village cable goes into a USB Ethernet adapter on the micro-USB
# OTG port (an OTG cable or hub). Drivers for the common adapters:
#   cdc-ether = generic, rtl8152 = Realtek RTL8152/8153, asix = AX88772, asix-ax88179 = AX88179
DEV_PACKAGES='kmod-usb-net-cdc-ether kmod-usb-net-rtl8152 kmod-usb-net-asix kmod-usb-net-asix-ax88179'

# The only fixed MAC on the board is the radio's: the name (feeder-xxxx) and address come from it,
# not from the USB adapter, which can be swapped.
MACDEV='phy0'

# Its Wi-Fi driver has no automatic channel choice (ACS): a fixed 2.4 GHz channel.
CH2='6'

# Wi-Fi firmware: not shipped by OpenWrt (licence unclear), so the build fetches it from the
# Raspberry Pi firmware repository, at a fixed commit, and checks each file. Two chip variants exist
# (43436 and 43436s): both go in, the driver picks the right one.
# Images that contain it are for the village's own units: don't put them in a public kit.
FW_URL='https://github.com/RPi-Distro/firmware-nonfree/raw/c91cd2804cf7463aab913e7247c176049f16bbd6/debian/config/brcm80211/brcm'
FW_DIR='lib/firmware/brcm'
FW_FILES='
510a7dd1e056199b309425548ee0bd846993a1837ac7fa1e4d3e641f05a1327a  brcmfmac43436-sdio.bin
4cda90facd8844cff60d80b34b24ecbae76adb9a62508a109461b8bf42b478d1  brcmfmac43436-sdio.txt
fce7cbb62ffa6a5a65ca97b13f6fbf28d06c02d986c2072d65bf72164755fc34  brcmfmac43436-sdio.clm_blob
68b9bcc9855d91733cd44c21de4cb507c91b0d32d838c0696def5eb96c99e2de  brcmfmac43436s-sdio.bin
37a8b85a5a9742761101b764a07bc4d0c8b09f2e180eaea3b503a834277ad595  brcmfmac43436s-sdio.txt
'
devices/rpi-zero-2-w/files/etc/uci-defaults/20-sparrows-device
#!/bin/sh
# Sparrows-Network: Raspberry Pi Zero 2 W. Runs after the common base, before the role.
# No Ethernet on the board: OpenWrt puts the Wi-Fi in the LAN bridge. Here the bridge gets the USB
# Ethernet adapter (eth0) instead; the role adds its Wi-Fi to the bridge itself.
# Name and address both come from the radio's MAC (MACDEV in device.conf), not the adapter's.
# Status: NOT YET TESTED.

BR=$(uci show network | sed -n "s/^network\.\([^.]*\)\.name='br-lan'$/\1/p")
if [ -z "$BR" ]; then
	BR='brlan'
	uci set network.$BR=device
	uci set network.$BR.name='br-lan'
	uci set network.$BR.type='bridge'
fi
uci -q delete network.$BR.ports
uci add_list network.$BR.ports='eth0'
uci set network.lan.device='br-lan'
# The bridge takes the radio's MAC, so the village address comes from the same MAC as the name
MAC=$(cat /sys/class/ieee80211/phy0/macaddress 2>/dev/null)
[ -z "$MAC" ] || uci set network.$BR.macaddr="$MAC"
uci commit network

exit 0
laptop on the village cable, then the Pi
# Before the first boot: the factory image written with ./build.sh sd. A USB Ethernet adapter in the
# port marked USB (not PWR), through an OTG cable or hub, and the village cable in the adapter.
# Find it from a laptop on that cable. Its name and address come from the radio's MAC, not the adapter's.
ping -6 -c2 ff02::1%eth0; ip -6 neigh show dev eth0
ssh root@fe80::<from the radio MAC>%eth0

# On the Pi (hostname feeder-<last 4 hex of the radio MAC>)
dmesg | grep -i brcmfmac        # "using brcm/brcmfmac43436-sdio" (or 43436s): firmware found
iw dev                          # one AP, channel 6
bridge link                     # eth0 (the adapter) and the Wi-Fi, both in br-lan
ip -6 addr show br-lan          # fe80:: from the radio MAC (+ fd7e:4c1a:9b20:1:... once a nest sends RAs)
ps | grep -e dnsmasq -e odhcpd  # nothing

# From a phone on the SSID: it joins, gets an address from the nest, reaches http://sparrows.lan/

# Undo: write the SD card again, with another image or with Raspberry Pi OS

5. Hedge image: feeder and perch in one box, example: EAP225-Outdoor5. Image de haie : mangeoire et perchoir dans un boîtier, exemple : EAP225-Outdoor5. Imagen de seto: comedero y percha en una caja, ejemplo: EAP225-Outdoor not yet tested

The EAP225-Outdoor has two radios, so one box can both let people in and carry the village. The hedge image gives each radio a job:

L'EAP225-Outdoor a deux radios : un seul boîtier peut donc à la fois faire entrer les gens et porter le village. L'image de haie donne un travail à chaque radio :

El EAP225-Outdoor tiene dos radios, así que una sola caja puede a la vez dejar entrar a la gente y llevar el pueblo. La imagen de seto da un trabajo a cada radio:

  • 5 GHz: the open Sparrows Wi-Fi on channel 132, exactly as on a feeder. Most phones use this one.
  • 2.4 GHz: an 802.11s mesh, sparrows-hedge, on channel 1, encrypted with SAE, carrying batman-adv to the neighbouring hedges. The same radio also serves the open Sparrows Wi-Fi for devices that only have 2.4 GHz.
  • Ethernet: bridged as on every station. A hedge cabled to a site switch joins the perches and nests on it.
  • 5 GHz : le Wi-Fi ouvert Sparrows sur le canal 132, exactement comme sur une mangeoire. La plupart des téléphones utilisent celui-là.
  • 2,4 GHz : un mesh 802.11s, sparrows-hedge, sur le canal 1, chiffré en SAE, qui transporte batman-adv vers les haies voisines. La même radio sert aussi le Wi-Fi ouvert Sparrows pour les appareils qui n'ont que le 2,4 GHz.
  • Ethernet : ponté comme sur chaque station. Une haie câblée à un switch de site rejoint les perchoirs et les nids qui y sont.
  • 5 GHz: el Wi-Fi abierto Sparrows en el canal 132, exactamente como en un comedero. La mayoría de los teléfonos usa este.
  • 2,4 GHz: una malla 802.11s, sparrows-hedge, en el canal 1, cifrada con SAE, que lleva batman-adv a los setos vecinos. La misma radio sirve también el Wi-Fi abierto Sparrows para los aparatos que solo tienen 2,4 GHz.
  • Ethernet: puenteado como en todas las estaciones. Un seto cableado a un switch de sitio se une a las perchas y a los nidos que haya en él.

Why 802.11s on 2.4 GHz: it is the only band where 802.11s works for us (never on DFS channels), and a mesh needs no AP and client roles, so there is nothing to set per unit and no radar wait on the backbone side. It is also how Freifunk communities run this kind of box. It serves P3 (any hedge relays, losing one loses nothing) and "works for amateurs" (flash, power, done).

Pourquoi 802.11s en 2,4 GHz : c'est la seule bande où 802.11s marche pour nous (jamais sur les canaux DFS), et un mesh n'a pas de rôles AP et client, donc rien à régler par boîtier et pas d'attente radar côté dorsale. C'est aussi ainsi que les communautés Freifunk font tourner ce genre de boîtier. Cela sert P3 (chaque haie relaie, en perdre une ne fait rien perdre) et « marche pour des amateurs » (flasher, alimenter, c'est fini).

Por qué 802.11s en 2,4 GHz: es la única banda donde 802.11s nos funciona (nunca en canales DFS), y una malla no necesita papeles de AP y cliente, así que no hay nada que ajustar por unidad ni espera de radar en el lado de la troncal. Es también como las comunidades Freifunk usan este tipo de caja. Sirve a P3 (cualquier seto retransmite, perder uno no hace perder nada) y a «funciona para aficionados» (flashear, alimentar, listo).

idea Not chosen for now: the EAP joining a CPE510 flyway on 5 GHz as a client, with the open Wi-Fi on the same radio. The open Wi-Fi would then sit on the flyway's channel and eat its airtime, and an access point next to a client on a DFS channel is untested ground.

idea Pas retenu pour l'instant : l'EAP qui rejoint une voie de CPE510 en 5 GHz comme client, avec le Wi-Fi ouvert sur la même radio. Le Wi-Fi ouvert serait alors sur le canal de la voie et lui prendrait du temps d'antenne, et un point d'accès à côté d'un client sur un canal DFS, c'est un terrain non testé.

idea No elegido por ahora: el EAP uniéndose como cliente a una ruta de CPE510 en 5 GHz, con el Wi-Fi abierto en la misma radio. El Wi-Fi abierto estaría entonces en el canal de la ruta y le quitaría tiempo de aire, y un punto de acceso junto a un cliente en un canal DFS es terreno sin probar.

First boot: the shared file in What each port and radio does, with this line of uses.txt: hedge wire flyway+feeder feeder -.

Premier démarrage : le fichier commun de Ce que fait chaque port et chaque radio, avec cette ligne de uses.txt : hedge wire flyway+feeder feeder -.

Primer arranque: el archivo común de Lo que hace cada puerto y cada radio, con esta línea de uses.txt: hedge wire flyway+feeder feeder -.

install, verify, undo
# Find it like a feeder, from a laptop on its cable
ping -6 -c2 ff02::1%eth0; ip -6 neigh show dev eth0
ssh root@fe80::<from MAC>%eth0

# On the hedge (hostname hedge-<last 4 hex of the MAC>)
iw dev | grep -e Interface -e type -e channel   # 2.4 GHz: mesh point + AP, ch 1; 5 GHz: AP, ch 132
logread | grep -i -e cac -e mesh                # DFS-CAC-COMPLETED on 5 GHz; mesh peering on 2.4 GHz
batctl if                       # the 2.4 GHz mesh interface: active
batctl n                        # the other hedges in radio range
batctl o                        # every batman node of the village
batctl bl                       # bridge loop avoidance: enabled
batctl tp <other hedge MAC>     # throughput between two hedges
ps | grep -e dnsmasq -e odhcpd  # nothing

# From a laptop on the SSID: only the nests should answer
rdisc6 wlan0
ip -6 route | grep default      # default via the nest's fe80 only (seen 2026-11-14)
ip -6 addr show wlan0           # SLAAC addresses from the nest's prefixes

# Undo
firstboot && reboot             # back to this image's defaults
# Or flash the feeder image: same box, open Wi-Fi only

Added on 2026-11-19. A tree on OpenWrt. It joins the open Sparrows Wi-Fi as a client, like a phone, gets its address from the nests' RAs, and runs rnsd with transport on, so it relays Reticulum traffic for the others. It bridges nothing, sends no RA, serves no on-ramp. On a Pi Zero 2 W it needs no USB adapter: plug it in where the Wi-Fi reaches.

Ajouté le 2026-11-19. Un arbre sous OpenWrt. Il rejoint le Wi-Fi ouvert Sparrows comme client, comme un téléphone, prend son adresse dans les RA des nids, et fait tourner rnsd avec le transport activé : il relaie le trafic Reticulum pour les autres. Il ne fait aucun pont, n'envoie aucun RA, ne sert aucune rampe d'accès. Sur un Pi Zero 2 W, pas besoin d'adaptateur USB : on le branche là où le Wi-Fi arrive.

Añadido el 2026-11-19. Un árbol con OpenWrt. Se une al Wi-Fi abierto Sparrows como cliente, igual que un teléfono, toma su dirección de los RA de los nidos y ejecuta rnsd con el transporte activado, así que reenvía el tráfico Reticulum de los demás. No hace de puente, no envía RA, no sirve ninguna rampa de acceso. En una Pi Zero 2 W no necesita adaptador USB: se enchufa donde llegue el Wi-Fi.

Reticulum is not an OpenWrt package. build.sh downloads its wheel at a pinned version (tree/role.conf: version and sha256), checks it, and unpacks it into the image, so the tree needs no Internet and no pip. Python and the two libraries Reticulum needs come from OpenWrt. rnsd runs as its own user, not root, and its identity survives updates. The config was checked on 2026-11-19 with rnsd 1.6.1 on a laptop (it starts, the interface waits for wsta); the image itself has not run yet. It needs room: SD-card devices only, never 8 or 16 MB of flash.

Reticulum n'est pas un paquet OpenWrt. build.sh télécharge sa wheel à une version figée (tree/role.conf : version et sha256), la vérifie et la déballe dans l'image : l'arbre n'a besoin ni d'Internet ni de pip. Python et les deux bibliothèques dont Reticulum a besoin viennent d'OpenWrt. rnsd tourne sous son propre utilisateur, pas root, et son identité survit aux mises à jour. La configuration a été vérifiée le 2026-11-19 avec rnsd 1.6.1 sur un portable (il démarre, l'interface attend wsta) ; l'image elle-même n'a pas encore tourné. Il faut de la place : appareils à carte SD seulement, jamais 8 ou 16 Mo de flash.

Reticulum no es un paquete de OpenWrt. build.sh descarga su wheel en una versión fijada (tree/role.conf: versión y sha256), la comprueba y la desempaqueta en la imagen, así que el árbol no necesita ni Internet ni pip. Python y las dos bibliotecas que Reticulum necesita vienen de OpenWrt. rnsd funciona con su propio usuario, no como root, y su identidad sobrevive a las actualizaciones. La configuración se comprobó el 2026-11-19 con rnsd 1.6.1 en un portátil (arranca, la interfaz espera a wsta); la imagen en sí aún no ha funcionado. Necesita espacio: solo aparatos con tarjeta SD, nunca 8 o 16 MB de flash.

tree/role.conf

# that runs Reticulum (the tree role, or a reticulum service in uses.txt or a manual line).
# Python wheels, pinned by version and sha256, unpacked into /usr/lib/sparrows/python in the image,
# so the unit needs no Internet and no pip. A new version = a new line here, after a bench test.
#   rns + lxmf + nomadnet: Reticulum, messages, the page node
#   urwid, wcwidth, qrcode, typing_extensions: what nomadnet imports (wcwidth 0.8.5: the last pure-Python one)
WHEELS='

First boot: the shared file in What each port and radio does, with this line of uses.txt: tree wire join join reticulum.

Premier démarrage : le fichier commun de Ce que fait chaque port et chaque radio, avec cette ligne de uses.txt : tree wire join join reticulum.

Primer arranque: el archivo común de Lo que hace cada puerto y cada radio, con esta línea de uses.txt: tree wire join join reticulum.

On a tree+feeder unit (or a nest) no radio joins, so Reticulum runs on the village bridge, br-lan, instead of wsta: first boot writes the right one in the config below.

Sur un boîtier tree+feeder (ou un nid) aucune radio ne rejoint, donc Reticulum tourne sur le pont du village, br-lan, au lieu de wsta : le premier démarrage écrit le bon dans la config ci-dessous.

En una unidad tree+feeder (o un nido) ninguna radio se une, así que Reticulum funciona en el puente del pueblo, br-lan, en lugar de wsta: el primer arranque escribe el correcto en la configuración de abajo.

tree/files/etc/reticulum/config
# Sparrows-Network: Reticulum transport node (tree, or any unit with the reticulum service).
# Status: NOT YET TESTED on OpenWrt.
[reticulum]
  enable_transport = Yes          # a tree relays for the others
  share_instance = Yes            # rns status and the other tools use this rnsd
  panic_on_interface_error = No

[logging]
  loglevel = 2                    # errors only: a tree remembers as little as possible

[interfaces]
  # The village. First boot puts the interface here: wsta (the Wi-Fi client) when a radio joins the
  # Sparrows Wi-Fi, br-lan (the village bridge: wire and feeders) when none does
  # No group_id: Reticulum's default group, the one Sideband and the other apps use out of the box.
  # Every device of the village must be in the same group, or they never see each other (seen
  # 2026-11-21: a laptop with group_id = sparrows and the nest found 0 peers)
  [[Sparrows]]
    type = AutoInterface
    enabled = yes
    devices = @RNSDEV@
tree/files/etc/init.d/rnsd
#!/bin/sh /etc/rc.common
# Sparrows-Network tree: Reticulum transport daemon, as user rns, restarted if it stops.

START=95
STOP=10
USE_PROCD=1

start_service() {
	procd_open_instance
	procd_set_param env PYTHONPATH=/usr/lib/sparrows/python
	procd_set_param command /usr/bin/python3 -m RNS.Utilities.rnsd --config /etc/reticulum -s
	procd_set_param user rns
	procd_set_param respawn 3600 5 0
	procd_close_instance
}
tree/files/usr/bin/rns
#!/bin/sh
# rns: the Reticulum tools on a Sparrows tree, with its config.
#   rns status          interfaces and traffic (rnstatus)
#   rns path <hash>     is there a path to this destination (rnpath)
#   rns probe <...>     (rnprobe)
[ -n "$1" ] || { sed -n '2,5s/^# \{0,1\}//p' "$0"; exit 1; }
TOOL=$1; shift
PYTHONPATH=/usr/lib/sparrows/python exec /usr/bin/python3 -m "RNS.Utilities.rn$TOOL" --config /etc/reticulum "$@"

The Sparrows page, served by every treeLa page Sparrows, servie par chaque arbreLa página Sparrows, servida por cada árbol not yet tested

Added on 2026-11-19. Each tree also runs Nomad Network in node mode, so anyone with a Reticulum app (Nomad Network, MeshChat) can read a short page about Sparrows-Network: what it is, how to join, the birds, the rules we keep, how to help. It is in English, French and Spanish, with links between them, and offers this handbook as a file to download. The tree announces itself as Sparrows-Network (tree-xxxx) every six hours. It stores no messages: that stays on the nests (lxmd). A tree is a relay first; it serves this page because it has room, as the tree card allows.

Ajouté le 2026-11-19. Chaque arbre fait aussi tourner Nomad Network en mode nœud : toute personne qui a une appli Reticulum (Nomad Network, MeshChat) peut lire une courte page sur Sparrows-Network : ce que c'est, comment rejoindre, les oiseaux, les règles qu'on garde, comment aider. Elle est en anglais, français et espagnol, avec des liens entre elles, et propose ce manuel en fichier à télécharger. L'arbre s'annonce comme Sparrows-Network (tree-xxxx) toutes les six heures. Il ne garde aucun message : ça reste sur les nids (lxmd). Un arbre est d'abord un relais ; il sert cette page parce qu'il a la place, comme le permet la carte arbre.

Añadido el 2026-11-19. Cada árbol ejecuta además Nomad Network en modo nodo, así que cualquiera con una app de Reticulum (Nomad Network, MeshChat) puede leer una página corta sobre Sparrows-Network: qué es, cómo unirse, los pájaros, las reglas que mantenemos, cómo ayudar. Está en inglés, francés y español, con enlaces entre ellas, y ofrece este manual como archivo para descargar. El árbol se anuncia como Sparrows-Network (tree-xxxx) cada seis horas. No guarda mensajes: eso se queda en los nidos (lxmd). Un árbol es ante todo un relé; sirve esta página porque tiene sitio, como permite la tarjeta árbol.

The pages are Micron, Nomad Network's markup, in the build tree: index.mu (English, below), fr.mu and es.mu next to it. Change all three together. Nomad Network and the libraries it needs come as pinned wheels, like Reticulum. Checked on 2026-11-19 on a laptop, from the files build.sh put in the image: the node started and served the three pages and the handbook file to a second Reticulum program. Not yet run on OpenWrt.

Les pages sont en Micron, le balisage de Nomad Network, dans l'arbre de build : index.mu (anglais, ci-dessous), fr.mu et es.mu à côté. On change les trois ensemble. Nomad Network et les bibliothèques dont il a besoin viennent en wheels figées, comme Reticulum. Vérifié le 2026-11-19 sur un portable, à partir des fichiers que build.sh a mis dans l'image : le nœud a démarré et servi les trois pages et le fichier du manuel à un second programme Reticulum. Pas encore lancé sous OpenWrt.

Las páginas están en Micron, el marcado de Nomad Network, en el árbol de construcción: index.mu (inglés, abajo), fr.mu y es.mu al lado. Se cambian las tres juntas. Nomad Network y las bibliotecas que necesita vienen como wheels fijadas, como Reticulum. Comprobado el 2026-11-19 en un portátil, con los archivos que build.sh puso en la imagen: el nodo arrancó y sirvió las tres páginas y el archivo del manual a un segundo programa Reticulum. Aún no se ha lanzado en OpenWrt.

tree/files/usr/share/sparrows/nomadnet/pages/index.mu
#!c=3600
>Sparrows-Network

`c`!Standalone Peer Access Radio Relay Over Wireless Stations`!
`a
`[English`:/page/index.mu]  ·  `[Français`:/page/fr.mu]  ·  `[Español`:/page/es.mu]
`a

-

A community network that runs without the Internet. Fixed Wi-Fi stations carry it across the village, and later from one village to the next.

You reached this page over it: it was served by a small box nearby, a tree or a nest.

>>Join

`!1.`! Connect to the open Wi-Fi `!Sparrows`!. No password, no account.
`!2.`! Open `!http://sparrows.lan/`! in a browser: the handbook, the Sparrows Tao.
`!3.`! To write to others, use a Reticulum app: Sideband on Android, MeshChat or Nomad Network on a laptop. Your app makes your key. Your key is your address.

>>How it works

Stations are named after birds:
 `!nest`!     the village's small server: addresses, the welcome page, a mailbox for messages
 `!perch`!    a radio on a mast; two perches make a `!flyway`! between places
 `!feeder`!   open Wi-Fi where people connect
 `!hedge`!    a feeder that also links to the next hedges by radio
 `!tree`!     a relay anyone can plant: it passes on what it hears

>>The rules we keep

 - No Internet on the network, no account, no logs.
 - You are your key, not a login an admin can delete.
 - A commons: anyone can add a station or a tree. Each village holds its own keys.

>>Help

Plant a tree, put up a feeder, or bring a mast. Everything is in the handbook.
`[Download the handbook (HTML file)`:/file/sparrows-tao.html]
tree/files/etc/nomadnetwork/config
# Sparrows-Network tree: Nomad Network in node mode, serving the Sparrows page. No client, no UI.
# Status: NOT YET TESTED on OpenWrt.
[logging]
  loglevel = 2                    # errors only
  destination = file

[client]
  enable_client = no
  announce_at_start = no

[node]
  enable_node = yes
  node_name = Sparrows-Network (@NAME@)       # first boot puts the unit's name here
  announce_at_start = yes
  announce_interval = 360         # minutes: every 6 hours
  pages_path = /usr/share/sparrows/nomadnet/pages
  files_path = /usr/share/sparrows/nomadnet/files
  disable_propagation = yes       # message storage stays on the nests (lxmd)
tree/files/etc/init.d/nomadnet
#!/bin/sh /etc/rc.common
# Sparrows-Network tree: Nomad Network page node (the Sparrows page), as user rns.
# It uses the rnsd started just before it (shared instance).

START=96
STOP=09
USE_PROCD=1

start_service() {
	procd_open_instance
	procd_set_param env PYTHONPATH=/usr/lib/sparrows/python HOME=/etc/nomadnetwork
	procd_set_param command /usr/bin/python3 -m nomadnet.nomadnet --daemon \
		--config /etc/nomadnetwork --rnsconfig /etc/reticulum
	procd_set_param user rns
	procd_set_param respawn 3600 10 0
	procd_close_instance
}

vi /etc/sparrows-pages/index.mu

/etc/init.d/nomadnet restart

# Undo the try: put pages_path back to /usr/share/sparrows/nomadnet/pages, restart, rm -r /etc/sparrows-pages
laptop on the village, then the tree
# Find it from a laptop on the village Wi-Fi or cable. Name and address come from the radio's MAC.
ping -6 -c2 ff02::1%<if>; ip -6 neigh show dev <if>
ssh root@<its fe80:: or fd7e:4c1a:9b20:1:... address>

# On the tree (hostname tree-<last 4 hex of the radio MAC>)

ip -6 addr show wsta             # fe80:: + SLAAC addresses from the nests' prefixes, no inet

logread | grep -i python         # nothing (errors would show here)
ps | grep -e dnsmasq -e odhcpd   # nothing
